     1	import { RECEIPT_POLICY, type ReceiptPolicy } from './policy.js';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * **What may differ between a quote and the text it was taken from.**
     5	 *
     6	 * ## Why this is a policy and not a `.replace()` chain
     7	 *
     8	 * Until r5 one function — `normalizeForMatch` — served two jobs with opposite
     9	 * risk profiles. It decided which model reads a window (routing), and it decided
    10	 * whether a quote and a message are the same text (the receipt). Getting routing
    11	 * wrong costs one model call. Getting the receipt wrong mints something false
    12	 * about a named person. One function cannot be tuned for both, and the version
    13	 * that was tuned for routing was doing measured semantic damage on the receipt
    14	 * path — r4's blind review found three separate cuts:
    15	 *
    16	 * | it deleted            | so this became certifiable                                        |
    17	 * | --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
    18	 * | backticks             | `` `Deploy production Friday.` `` — a sample — as its author's assertion |
    19	 * | link destinations     | `[https://safe.example](https://evil.example)` accepted as naming the safe URL |
    20	 * | NFKC compatibility    | `ｅｖｉｌ.example` and `evil.example` as one hostname               |
    21	 *
    22	 * Each deletion was individually defensible ("a model drops formatting while
    23	 * quoting correctly") and collectively it meant the receipt was comparing two
    24	 * texts neither author wrote.
    25	 *
    26	 * `packages/ingest/src/text.ts` had already answered the same question the other
    27	 * way for message bodies — stored verbatim, because "determinism does not need
    28	 * normalisation" and round 1's NFC pass was lossy. This file makes the core
    29	 * agree with the corpus.
    30	 *
    31	 * ## The shape of the answer
    32	 *
    33	 * Not a list of things to delete. **A list of the differences a quote is allowed
    34	 * to have from its source, each with the argument that admits it.** This is the
    35	 * third arrival of that principle in this repo (`RETRO.md`: denylists of
    36	 * evasions are unbounded; allowlist the compliant forms), and it is the same move
    37	 * `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens` made for the bearing check.
    38	 *
    39	 * The bar for an entry is the bar r4 set for `droppableTokens`: **an argument
    40	 * nobody can break.** Two candidates failed it and are recorded here so they are
    41	 * not re-added:
    42	 *
    43	 *  - **Case folding.** `US` / `us`, `Bill` / `bill`, `March` / `march` are
    44	 *    different words, and a quote is supposed to be verbatim. A model that
    45	 *    re-cases a sentence has edited it, and an edited reading goes to a person.
    46	 *  - **Underscore emphasis.** `_x_` is Markdown emphasis and `__init__` is an
    47	 *    identifier, and no rule separates them. Asterisk emphasis stays, because
    48	 *    `*` is not an identifier character in anything this product reads.
    49	 */
    50	
    51	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    52	 * Emptiness
    53	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
    54	
    55	/**
    56	 * **Is there anything here at all?** — the one emptiness test, used before every
    57	 * required receipt input.
    58	 *
    59	 * r3's gauntlet, as a polish note that is really a principle: zero-width
    60	 * characters were refused only *incidentally*. `"​"` is not `""`, so it walked
    61	 * past every `trim()` check marked "required" and died three checks later as
    62	 * not-found or too-short. An accidental refusal is one code change away from an
    63	 * accidental acceptance.
    64	 *
    65	 * The fix is not a list of invisible characters — that list is unbounded in
    66	 * exactly the way a stopword list is. It is the complement: **content is a
    67	 * letter, a digit, or a pictograph.**
    68	 *
    69	 * **The pictograph is r5.** Until r5 this read `[\p{L}\p{N}]`, and a message
    70	 * that is a row of 🚫 reduced to "nothing" — so `proposal.ts` refused it as a
    71	 * *blank quote* and the acceptance engine refused its window as *absent*. Both
    72	 * refusals were right about the outcome and lying about the cause, which is the
    73	 * shape r4's tokenizer defect had with Cyrillic: a failure wearing another
    74	 * failure's clothes. It also contradicted this package's own tokenizer, which
    75	 * has said since r4 that "every script, every mark and every emoji is content".
    76	 * Two answers to "is this text?" in one package is one answer too many.
    77	 *
    78	 * Everything else — spaces of every width, zero-width joiners, format and
    79	 * control codes, unassigned code points, lone combining marks, punctuation on
    80	 * its own — is still absence, whether or not anybody has enumerated it. A quote
    81	 * of `"…"`, a message body of `"​"`, and `""` are the same fact about the world
    82	 * and get the same answer.
    83	 */
    84	const CONTENTFUL = /[\p{L}\p{N}\p{Extended_Pictographic}]/u;
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * True when `text` carries at least one letter, digit or pictograph.
    88	 *
    89	 * A type predicate, and that is r5 too: `tsc` refuses `fix/core-engine-r4` as
    90	 * committed, on two errors this narrowing is half of. `commitmentAttribution`
    91	 * guarded a `string | null | undefined` with `isBlank` and then handed it to a
    92	 * function taking `string`, which reads correctly and does not compile — the
    93	 * guard proves the value is a string and the signature threw the proof away.
    94	 */
    95	export function hasContent(text: string | null | undefined): text is string {
    96	  return text !== null && text !== undefined && CONTENTFUL.test(text);
    97	}
    98	
    99	/** `hasContent`, negated — reads better at the gates, which are all refusals. */
   100	export function isBlank(text: string | null | undefined): boolean {
   101	  return !hasContent(text);
   102	}
   103	
   104	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   105	 * The allowlist
   106	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   107	
   108	/**
   109	 * Format and control characters, which render as nothing and must not survive
   110	 * into a comparison. `\p{Cf}` covers U+200B–U+200F, U+2060–U+2064, U+00AD and
   111	 * U+FEFF; `\p{Cc}` covers the C0/C1 controls. Whitespace of every width is
   112	 * handled by `\s`, which in JavaScript already includes NBSP, the en/em spaces
   113	 * and U+3000.
   114	 *
   115	 * Admitted because a character with no rendering cannot carry an assertion:
   116	 * deleting them maps texts a reader sees as identical onto each other, and it
   117	 * cannot map two texts a reader sees as *different* onto one — a bidi override
   118	 * that reverses a sentence is deleted, and the reversed bytes stay reversed, so
   119	 * the comparison still refuses.
   120	 */
   121	const INVISIBLE = /[\p{Cf}\p{Cc}]/gu;
   122	
   123	/**
   124	 * A **paired** run of asterisks around a non-empty span — Markdown emphasis, in
   125	 * the only form Markdown itself accepts.
   126	 *
   127	 * Paired rather than "any asterisk", so `2 * 3 * 4` survives: an unpaired
   128	 * asterisk is arithmetic or a glob, not a typeface. Admitted because emphasis
   129	 * changes how a sentence is set, never who, whether, how many or when.
   130	 */
   131	const PAIRED_EMPHASIS = /(\*{1,3})(?=\S)([\s\S]*?\S)\1/g;
   132	
   133	/**
   134	 * `[text](destination)`, and the image form.
   135	 *
   136	 * **The destination is content.** r4's blind review: a message reading
   137	 * `Use [https://safe.example/app](https://evil.example/app) today.` normalized
   138	 * to a sentence naming the *safe* URL, so a statement naming the safe URL was
   139	 * certifiable against a record whose actionable link goes elsewhere. That is a
   140	 * security defect, not a fidelity one — the reader clicks the record, not the
   141	 * statement.
   142	 *
   143	 * So a link normalizes to its text **and** its destination, and the destination
   144	 * only disappears when the text already states it. A statement that drops the
   145	 * destination is then a statement that drops words the quote carries, which is
   146	 * the case `refer` exists for: a person looks at where the link actually goes.
   147	 */
   148	const MARKDOWN_LINK = /!?\[([^\]]*)\]\(\s*([^)\s]*)(?:\s+"[^"]*")?\s*\)/g;
   149	
   150	/**
   151	 * Code, in every spelling Markdown gives it: a fence, a double-backtick span, a
   152	 * single-backtick span.
   153	 *
   154	 * Split on rather than folded away, because the prose rules above are wrong
   155	 * inside code — `*` is a glob, `_` is an identifier, `[a](b)` is a literal — and
   156	 * because **the backticks themselves are content**. A backticked span is
   157	 * *mention, not use*: `` `Deploy production Friday.` `` is a string its author
   158	 * displayed, not a sentence its author asserted, and r4's normalization deleted
   159	 * exactly the two characters that carry that distinction. `~~` was excluded from
   160	 * folding in r4 for the same class of reason (strikethrough is retraction, not
   161	 * emphasis); this is that argument applied to the delimiter that separates
   162	 * quoting a thing from saying it.
   163	 */
   164	const CODE_SPAN = /(```[\s\S]*?```|``[\s\S]*?``|`[^`\n]*`)/;
   165	
   166	/**
   167	 * The differences a quote may have from the text it was taken from — **and no
   168	 * others.**
   169	 *
   170	 * In order, with the argument that admits each:
   171	 *
   172	 *  1. **Whitespace runs collapse, and the ends are trimmed.** A client wraps a
   173	 *     message and a quote copied out of it carries different line breaks. A run
   174	 *     of horizontal space is not a token in any script.
   175	 *  2. **Characters with no rendering are dropped.** See `INVISIBLE`.
   176	 *  3. **A typographic apostrophe is an apostrophe.** `won’t` and `won't` are one
   177	 *     word entered two ways; treating them as two produces a refusal that has
   178	 *     nothing to do with what was said.
   179	 *  4. **Paired asterisk emphasis may be absent.** See `PAIRED_EMPHASIS`.
   180	 *  5. **A link contributes its destination.** See `MARKDOWN_LINK`.
   181	 *
   182	 * Not admitted, and each one used to be: NFKC compatibility folding, case
   183	 * folding, backtick deletion, underscore emphasis, bare link-text substitution.
   184	 * The header of this file says why.
   185	 */
   186	export function normalizeForReceipt(text: string): string {
   187	  const out: string[] = [];
   188	  for (const [index, segment] of text.split(CODE_SPAN).entries()) {
   189	    // `String.split` with one capture group alternates: prose, delimiter, prose…
   190	    const isCode = index % 2 === 1;
   191	    out.push(isCode ? segment.replace(INVISIBLE, '') : foldProse(segment));
   192	  }
   193	  return out.join('').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
   194	}
   195	
   196	function foldProse(text: string): string {
   197	  let folded = text.replace(INVISIBLE, '').replace(/[’ʼ]/g, "'");
   198	  // Emphasis nests (`**bold *and italic* **`), so run to a fixed point rather
   199	  // than once. Bounded: every pass removes at least two characters.
   200	  for (let previous = ''; previous !== folded; ) {
   201	    previous = folded;
   202	    folded = folded.replace(PAIRED_EMPHASIS, '$2');
   203	  }
   204	  return folded.replace(MARKDOWN_LINK, (_match, label: string, destination: string) => {
   205	    const text = label.trim();
   206	    const target = destination.trim();
   207	    if (target.length === 0) return text;
   208	    // The destination disappears only when the text already states it, so an
   209	    // autolink does not turn into a stutter that nothing can bear.
   210	    return text === target ? text : `${text} ${target}`;
   211	  });
   212	}
   213	
   214	/**
   215	 * The lossy normalization, for the questions where being lossy is the safe
   216	 * direction: **which model reads this window**, and **is this reading a
   217	 * duplicate of one the room already accepted**.
   218	 *
   219	 * Everything `normalizeForReceipt` refuses to do is done here, on purpose. A
   220	 * trigger that fires on a fullwidth spelling costs one model call. A dedup that
   221	 * treats `Deploy` and `deploy` as one word discards a re-proposal, which is what
   222	 * it is for. Neither can mint a fact, and `escalation.ts` states in its own
   223	 * stopword comment that the routing list "has not decided whether a reading
   224	 * becomes a fact since r4, and it must never do so again".
   225	 *
   226	 * The one call this makes on the receipt path is the later-correction scan, and
   227	 * it is deliberate: that scan is a *detector*, its false positives cost a
   228	 * referral rather than an acceptance, so it wants the loose comparison.
   229	 */
   230	export function normalizeForRouting(text: string): string {
   231	  return text
   232	    .normalize('NFKC')
   233	    .replace(/[’ʼ]/g, "'")
   234	    .replace(MARKDOWN_LINK, '$1')
   235	    .replace(/[*_`]+/g, ' ')
   236	    .replace(INVISIBLE, '')
   237	    .replace(/\s+/g, ' ')
   238	    .trim()
   239	    .toLowerCase();
   240	}
   241	
   242	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   243	 * Tokens and sentences
   244	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   245	
   246	/**
   247	 * A word in any script: letters, digits and combining marks, with apostrophes
   248	 * *inside* it. Anything else visible is one token of its own.
   249	 *
   250	 * The alternation is the whole design. r4's first version split on
   251	 * `/[^a-z0-9']+/`, which is a **denylist of the characters that may carry
   252	 * meaning** wearing a tokenizer's clothes, and r4's own blind review walked
   253	 * through it four ways in one pass: `Bob will ｎｏｔ deploy` (fullwidth — not
   254	 * `[a-z]`, so deleted), `Bob will не deploy` (Cyrillic — deleted), `❌ Bob will
   255	 * deploy` (emoji — deleted), and `Bob will deploy Friday?` (the question mark —
   256	 * deleted). Each one bore its own affirmative and auto-accepted. A Russian
   257	 * sentence, meanwhile, tokenized to *nothing at all* and was refused as empty.
   258	 *
   259	 * So the rule is inverted: a token is a word, or it is a visible character, and
   260	 * `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens` is the only thing that may go missing.
   261	 */
   262	const TOKEN = /[\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}]+(?:'[\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}]+)*|[^\s']/gu;
   263	
   264	/**
   265	 * Every token of a text, in the order it was written.
   266	 *
   267	 * Deliberately unlike `contentTokens` in all four ways that matter, and each
   268	 * one is a defect the gauntlets exploited or could have:
   269	 *
   270	 *  - **No stopword list.** `not`, `all`, `some`, `will`, `might`, `unless` and
   271	 *    every word nobody has thought of yet are content here. There is no list to
   272	 *    get wrong because there is no list.
   273	 *  - **No de-duplication.** "not not" is two tokens. A `Set` cannot tell double
   274	 *    negation from single.
   275	 *  - **No length floor.** `no` is two characters and inverts a sentence.
   276	 *  - **No character class of "real" words.** Every script, every mark and every
   277	 *    emoji is content; see `TOKEN`.
   278	 *
   279	 * Apostrophes are kept *inside* a word, so `won't` is one token and is not
   280	 * `will`. A standalone apostrophe is dropped rather than tokenized — it is a
   281	 * quotation mark, `'online'` is the word `online`, and an apostrophe cannot
   282	 * negate, quantify, or change a modal.
   283	 */
   284	export function orderedTokens(text: string): string[] {
   285	  return normalizeForReceipt(text).match(TOKEN) ?? [];
   286	}
   287	
   288	/** `orderedTokens` under the lossy fold — for detectors, never for a receipt. */
   289	export function routingTokens(text: string): string[] {
   290	  return normalizeForRouting(text).match(TOKEN) ?? [];
   291	}
   292	
   293	/**
   294	 * A text split into sentences: on a terminator followed by space, or a newline.
   295	 *
   296	 * Deliberately crude, and crude in the safe direction — it splits *less* than a
   297	 * linguist would (an abbreviation keeps its sentence whole), and every check
   298	 * that uses it requires the quote to cover whole sentences, so under-splitting
   299	 * can only make that harder to satisfy.
   300	 */
   301	export function sentencesOf(text: string): string[] {
   302	  return text
   303	    .split(/(?<=[.!?…。？！])\s+|\n+/u)
   304	    .map((sentence) => sentence.trim())
   305	    .filter((sentence) => sentence.length > 0);
   306	}
   307	
   308	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   309	 * Does the quote bear the statement?
   310	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   311	
   312	/**
   313	 * What the bearing check found. **`borne` is the only affirmative answer** —
   314	 * every other shape of this result is a refusal, and the two lists say which
   315	 * refusal it is.
   316	 */
   317	export interface StatementBearing {
   318	  /**
   319	   * True only when the statement is the quote with nothing removed but
   320	   * `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens`, in the order it was written.
   321	   */
   322	  borne: boolean;
   323	  /** Tokens the statement asserts that the quote does not contain, in order. */
   324	  unmatchedInStatement: string[];
   325	  /** Tokens the quote contains that the statement drops, in order. */
   326	  unmatchedInQuote: string[];
   327	  /** Set when the check declined to run at all, rather than running and failing. */
   328	  undecidable: 'empty_quote' | 'empty_statement' | 'too_long' | null;
   329	}
   330	
   331	const declined = (undecidable: StatementBearing['undecidable']): StatementBearing => ({
   332	  borne: false,
   333	  unmatchedInStatement: [],
   334	  unmatchedInQuote: [],
   335	  undecidable,
   336	});
   337	
   338	/**
   339	 * The alignment itself, over tokens somebody else produced.
   340	 *
   341	 * Split out from `statementBearing` in r5 so the later-correction detector can
   342	 * run the same comparison over *routing* tokens without a second implementation
   343	 * of the alignment. One alignment, two tokenizations, and the caller says which
   344	 * — rather than two alignments that can drift.
   345	 *
   346	 * Greedy, with a one-sided lookahead so a single extra word resynchronises
   347	 * instead of scrambling the rest of the comparison. Deterministic, total, and
   348	 * bounded by `RECEIPT_POLICY.maxAlignedTokens` — an input too big to align is
   349	 * refused, not approximated.
   350	 */
   351	export function alignTokens(
   352	  q: readonly string[],
   353	  s: readonly string[],
   354	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   355	): StatementBearing {
   356	  if (q.length === 0) return declined('empty_quote');
   357	  if (s.length === 0) return declined('empty_statement');
   358	  if (q.length > policy.maxAlignedTokens || s.length > policy.maxAlignedTokens) {
   359	    return declined('too_long');
   360	  }
   361	
   362	  const droppable = (token: string | undefined): boolean =>
   363	    token !== undefined && policy.droppableTokens.has(token);
   364	
   365	  const unmatchedInQuote: string[] = [];
   366	  const unmatchedInStatement: string[] = [];
   367	  let i = 0;
   368	  let j = 0;
   369	
   370	  while (i < q.length && j < s.length) {
   371	    const qt = q[i];
   372	    const st = s[j];
   373	    if (qt === st) {
   374	      i += 1;
   375	      j += 1;
   376	      continue;
   377	    }
   378	    // A full stop on either side may be skipped silently — that is the entire
   379	    // licence this check grants, and `droppableTokens` says why.
   380	    if (droppable(qt)) {
   381	      i += 1;
   382	      continue;
   383	    }
   384	    if (droppable(st)) {
   385	      j += 1;
   386	      continue;
   387	    }
   388	    // A real disagreement. Resynchronise towards whichever side can still be
   389	    // matched, so one interposed word ("not") is reported as one interposed word
   390	    // rather than knocking every later token out of alignment.
   391	    if (st !== undefined && q.indexOf(st, i + 1) !== -1) {
   392	      if (qt !== undefined) unmatchedInQuote.push(qt);
   393	      i += 1;
   394	    } else {
   395	      if (st !== undefined) unmatchedInStatement.push(st);
   396	      j += 1;
   397	    }
   398	  }
   399	
   400	  // Whatever is left over on either side is unaccounted for — a trailing
   401	  // "unless CI is red" is exactly this case, and it is why the tail is not
   402	  // forgiven.
   403	  for (; i < q.length; i += 1) {
   404	    const token = q[i];
   405	    if (!droppable(token) && token !== undefined) unmatchedInQuote.push(token);
   406	  }
   407	  for (; j < s.length; j += 1) {
   408	    const token = s[j];
   409	    if (!droppable(token) && token !== undefined) unmatchedInStatement.push(token);
   410	  }
   411	
   412	  return {
   413	    borne: unmatchedInQuote.length === 0 && unmatchedInStatement.length === 0,
   414	    unmatchedInStatement,
   415	    unmatchedInQuote,
   416	    undecidable: null,
   417	  };
   418	}
   419	
   420	/**
   421	 * **Is the asserted statement a word-for-word reduction of this quote?**
   422	 *
   423	 * ## What this proves, stated in the terms it actually holds
   424	 *
   425	 * `borne === true` means exactly one thing, and it is worth writing out because
   426	 * the check it replaced claimed something it could not deliver:
   427	 *
   428	 * > Every token of the statement appears in the quote, in the same order, and
   429	 * > every token of the quote appears in the statement, in the same order, except
   430	 * > for the full stop in `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens`. A token is a word in
   431	 * > any script, or a visible mark, after the differences `normalizeForReceipt`
   432	 * > admits.
   433	 *
   434	 * That is a **structural** claim about two strings. It is not entailment, and
   435	 * nothing here can establish entailment — but combined with the checks around it
   436	 * (the quote is the whole of a cited author's own text in a message no later
   437	 * message revisits, and it is written by exactly one identifiable person) it
   438	 * supports the sentence the product actually needs to be able to say: *the
   439	 * sentence being asserted is one somebody in this room wrote, in these words,
   440	 * and nothing around it in the record changes it.*
   441	 *
   442	 * ## Why not the softer test
   443	 *
   444	 * r3 asked "how many of the statement's content words does the quote contain",
   445	 * over a de-duplicated set, with `not` dropped as a stopword. The answer for
   446	 * *"Bob will not deploy production Friday"* → *"Bob will deploy production
   447	 * Friday"* was 100%, and that acceptance was automatic. Every softening of the
   448	 * test above reopens it somewhere:
   449	 *
   450	 *  - A **set** cannot see order, so "A blocks B" and "B blocks A" are the same.
   451	 *  - A **threshold** licenses dropping whatever falls under it, and one dropped
   452	 *    word is all a negation needs.
   453	 *  - A **stopword list** decides in advance which words cannot matter, which is
   454	 *    the assumption that failed.
   455	 *  - Checking only the **covering span** of the statement inside the quote misses
   456	 *    the prefix ("I don't think ") and the suffix (" unless CI is red"), which is
   457	 *    where a qualifier lives. So the *whole* quote must be accounted for.
   458	 *
   459	 * The cost is stated rather than hidden: a model reading that paraphrases —
   460	 * expands a pronoun, fixes a tense, tightens the wording — is no longer
   461	 * auto-acceptable. It is not destroyed; it goes to a person, which is where a
   462	 * reading that is not in the record in these words belongs.
   463	 */
   464	export function statementBearing(
   465	  quote: string,
   466	  statement: string,
   467	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   468	): StatementBearing {
   469	  if (isBlank(quote)) return declined('empty_quote');
   470	  if (isBlank(statement)) return declined('empty_statement');
   471	  return alignTokens(orderedTokens(quote), orderedTokens(statement), policy);
   472	}
   473	
   474	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   475	 * Where the scissors may cut
   476	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   477	
   478	/** The quote's tokens, with the ones a receipt may lose taken out. */
   479	function significant(text: string, policy: ReceiptPolicy): string[] {
   480	  return orderedTokens(text).filter((token) => !policy.droppableTokens.has(token));
   481	}
   482	
   483	const sameRun = (a: readonly string[], b: readonly string[]): boolean =>
   484	  a.length === b.length && a.every((token, index) => token === b[index]);
   485	
   486	/**
   487	 * **Is the quote a run of whole sentences of this text, rather than a span cut
   488	 * out of the middle of one?**
   489	 *
   490	 * r4's own blind review found the defect this exists for. Making the *statement
   491	 * vs quote* comparison exact does nothing if the *quote vs message* relation is
   492	 * still "any substring", because the model chooses the span:
   493	 *
   494	 * | message body                                              | quote = statement                         |
   495	 * | --------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
   496	 * | It is not true that Bob will deploy production Friday …    | Bob will deploy production Friday …       |
   497	 * | Nobody thinks Bob will deploy production Friday this week   | Bob will deploy production Friday this week |
   498	 * | I doubt Bob will deploy production Friday this coming week  | Bob will deploy production Friday this coming week |
   499	 *
   500	 * Every one of those quotes is verbatim, correctly attributed, long enough, and
   501	 * borne word-for-word by its own statement — because the inverter was left
   502	 * outside the span. Quote-mining is the same defect as the stopword list with
   503	 * the scissors moved.
   504	 *
   505	 * This answers the narrower of the two questions r5 asks about the scissors;
   506	 * `quoteCoversOwnText` answers the other one, and the two report different
   507	 * problems because they tell a reader different things about the receipt.
   508	 */
   509	export function quoteSpansWholeSentences(
   510	  quote: string,
   511	  ownText: string,
   512	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   513	): boolean {
   514	  const wanted = significant(quote, policy);
   515	  if (wanted.length === 0) return false;
   516	
   517	  const sentences = sentencesOf(ownText);
   518	  // Bounded, and refusing rather than degrading, for the same reason the
   519	  // alignment is: the body is somebody else's input.
   520	  if (sentences.length > policy.maxScannedSentences) return false;
   521	  const tokenized = sentences.map((sentence) => significant(sentence, policy));
   522	
   523	  for (let start = 0; start < tokenized.length; start += 1) {
   524	    const run: string[] = [];
   525	    for (let end = start; end < tokenized.length; end += 1) {
   526	      run.push(...(tokenized[end] ?? []));
   527	      if (run.length > wanted.length) break;
   528	      if (run.length === wanted.length && sameRun(run, wanted)) return true;
   529	    }
   530	  }
   531	  return false;
   532	}
   533	
   534	/**
   535	 * **Is the quote the whole of what this author wrote here?**
   536	 *
   537	 * ## The residue r4 documented and then accepted anyway
   538	 *
   539	 * r4 closed the scissors *inside* a sentence and wrote the leftover down in its
   540	 * own prose: *"polarity that lives in a different sentence ('I will deploy
   541	 * Friday. Not.') is not visible to this and is not visible to any span rule …
   542	 * it is why the guarantee is written as 'somebody wrote this sentence', not
   543	 * 'somebody meant it'."*
   544	 *
   545	 * Every word of that is true and the code auto-accepted the sentence anyway,
   546	 * while the same round built the third severity the case needs. A limit written
   547	 * into a comment changes nothing about what the program does with an input that
   548	 * lands in it. So the limit is now a disposition: an input inside it is
   549	 * **referred**.
   550	 *
   551	 * ## Why the rule is "all of it" and not a list of inverters
   552	 *
   553	 * The obvious fix is to look at the neighbouring sentence for the things that
   554	 * change the force of the quoted one — a negation, `unless`, `if`, `was going
   555	 * to`, `instead`, a question mark, a retraction verb. That is a denylist, it is
   556	 * unbounded by construction, and this repo has now paid for that lesson three
   557	 * times (`RETRO.md`). Each candidate allowlist was tried and each one leaves the
   558	 * model holding the scissors:
   559	 *
   560	 * | candidate                                            | breaks on                                  |
   561	 * | ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
   562	 * | neighbour must be ≥ n words                          | `Unless CI is red.`                        |
   563	 * | neighbour must end in a full stop                    | `Not.`                                     |
   564	 * | neighbour must not open with a linking word          | a denylist of linking words, unbounded     |
   565	 * | quote must reach the end of the text                 | `Hypothetically. We will deploy Friday.`   |
   566	 *
   567	 * The only form with no scissors left in it is the one this function checks:
   568	 * **the quote is the entirety of the author's own text in the bearing message**
   569	 * (reply-blockquotes already removed, since those are somebody else's words).
   570	 * Then there is no neighbouring sentence, so there is nothing for a neighbouring
   571	 * sentence to do, and the guarantee strengthens from *somebody wrote this
   572	 * sentence* to *somebody wrote this message and it says exactly this*.
   573	 *
   574	 * The cost is stated rather than hidden, and it is large: a reading drawn from
   575	 * one sentence of a multi-sentence message is no longer auto-acceptable. It is
   576	 * not discarded — it is `refer`, so it stays staged with its quote beside its
   577	 * statement for a person to read, which is the disposition the whole third
   578	 * severity exists for.
   579	 */
   580	export function quoteCoversOwnText(
   581	  quote: string,
   582	  ownText: string,
   583	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   584	): boolean {
   585	  const wanted = significant(quote, policy);
   586	  if (wanted.length === 0) return false;
   587	  return sameRun(significant(ownText, policy), wanted);
   588	}
   589	
   590	/**
   591	 * **Is this text offered as an assertion?**
   592	 *
   593	 * A question quoted verbatim is an OpenQuestion or a referral, never a Claim.
   594	 * Until r5 nothing asked: `objects.ts` requires a nonempty string and the
   595	 * receipt proves string equality, so `"Would we deploy production Friday?"`
   596	 * minted as a `claim` with an identical quote auto-accepted — the receipt was
   597	 * perfect and the reading turned somebody's question into their position.
   598	 *
   599	 * The test is the mark, and it is the same argument `RECEIPT_POLICY`'s
   600	 * `droppableTokens` makes for keeping `?` out of the set it will forgive:
   601	 * *"Bob will deploy Friday?" is a question and minting it as an assertion is the
   602	 * same defect in different clothes.* Here the mark is read rather than compared.
   603	 *
   604	 * Deliberately only the mark. An interrogative without one ("I wonder whether we
   605	 * ship Friday") reads as an assertion to any string check, and inventing a
   606	 * grammar to catch it would be the guess this file refuses to make elsewhere.
   607	 * What is claimed is exactly what is checked: **a sentence carrying a question
   608	 * mark is not an assertion.**
   609	 */
   610	export function isAssertion(text: string): boolean {
   611	  return !orderedTokens(text).includes('?');
   612	}
   580	}
   581	
   582	function isWordChar(char: string | undefined): boolean {
   583	  return char !== undefined && /[a-z0-9]/.test(char);
   584	}
   585	
   586	function clip(text: string, limit = 120): string {
   587	  const flat = text.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
   588	  return flat.length <= limit ? flat : `${flat.slice(0, limit - 1)}…`;
   589	}
   590	
   591	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   592	 * Proposal validation — the checks that run *after* the model call
   593	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   594	
   595	/** A message as the provenance checker needs it. */
   596	export interface ProvenanceMessage {
   597	  id: string;
   598	  authorId: string;
   599	  body: string;
   600	}
   601	
   602	/**
   603	 * **Every way a receipt can be wrong, or unjudgeable — as data.**
   604	 *
   605	 * A `const` tuple rather than a bare union, because r4's blind review found the
   606	 * shape this closes: `acceptance.test.ts` carried a test titled "reaches every
   607	 * rule name the type declares" whose supposedly complete list *omitted* a rule,
   608	 * so the test passed while the engine behaviour it named could have been
   609	 * deleted. A restated list only catches removals; a derived one only catches
   610	 * additions. Deriving the type from the data catches both, because the list and
   611	 * the type are then the same object and a test can iterate it.
   612	 *
   613	 * What each kind means:
   614	 *
   615	 *  - `no_provenance` — a model proposal citing nothing. Schema-blocked on
   616	 *    `Proposal`, and checked again here because r2's gauntlet found the schema
   617	 *    was never reached: `appendEvent`/`reduce` folded whatever object they were
   618	 *    handed. A check that only runs one layer up is not a check.
   619	 *  - `unknown_message` — cites a message id that is not in the window.
   620	 *  - `missing_quote` — a model reading that quotes nothing. r3's gauntlet minted
   621	 *    a model *objective* with `quote: null` through the version of this that was
   622	 *    scoped to claims and commitments; it covers every model-minted type now.
   623	 *  - `quote_not_found` — the quote is in no cited message at all.
   624	 *  - `quote_only_in_reply_blockquote` — the quote is only in a cited message's
   625	 *    reply-blockquote, text the cited author did not write. The spike's worst
   626	 *    error, at 0.98 confidence.
   627	 *  - `elided_quote` — silently shortened with `…`, and not verbatim anywhere.
   628	 *  - `quote_too_short` — "yes", "ok", "+1" occur in every thread, so a citation
   629	 *    resting on one identifies the conversation rather than the sentence.
   630	 *  - `quote_does_not_bear_statement` — the sentence being minted asserts words
   631	 *    the quote does not contain. r2's gauntlet, major 1: cite Bob's unrelated
   632	 *    "yes", mint "Bob will deploy". Since r4 it also covers a quantifier or
   633	 *    modal *substitution* — quote "all services restart", mint "some services
   634	 *    restart".
   635	 *  - `quote_carries_more_than_statement` — every word of the statement is in the
   636	 *    quote, in order, and the quote says more. r3's gauntlet: quote "Bob will
   637	 *    **not** deploy production Friday", mint the affirmative. The dropped word
   638	 *    may be decorative or it may invert the sentence, and nothing here can tell
   639	 *    which.
   640	 *  - `quote_is_a_fragment` — a span cut out of the middle of a sentence rather
   641	 *    than a run of whole ones. r4's own blind review.
   642	 *  - `quote_omits_surrounding_text` — **r5.** The quote is whole sentences and
   643	 *    the author wrote more around them, so a neighbouring sentence this check
   644	 *    cannot read may change the force of the quoted one ("We will deploy
   645	 *    production Friday. Not."). r4 documented this as a residue it could not
   646	 *    see and auto-accepted it anyway; see `quoteCoversOwnText`.
   647	 *  - `superseded_by_later_message` — **r5.** A message later in the same window
   648	 *    restates the sentence with something added ("Correction: we will **not** …")
   649	 *    and the proposal cites only the earlier one. Append-only storage prevents
   650	 *    an edit; it does not make a correction part of acceptance.
   651	 *  - `statement_is_not_an_assertion` — **r5.** The sentence carries a question
   652	 *    mark and is being minted as something other than an open question. A
   653	 *    question quoted verbatim is an OpenQuestion or a referral, never a Claim.
   654	 *  - `statement_uncheckable` — the bearing check could not run: no quote, no
   655	 *    statement, or an input too large to align. Fails closed, because an
   656	 *    unchecked receipt is not a passed one.
   657	 *  - `ambiguous_quote` — two or more cited messages, by different people,
   658	 *    contain the quote. Taking the first in window order picks an author by
   659	 *    accident, and the author is the whole answer to "who said this".
   660	 *  - `attributed_person_not_author` — a Claim's claimant or a Commitment's owner
   661	 *    did not write the message bearing the quote.
   662	 */
   663	export const PROVENANCE_PROBLEM_KINDS = [
   664	  'no_provenance',
   665	  'unknown_message',
   666	  'missing_quote',
   667	  'quote_not_found',
   668	  'quote_only_in_reply_blockquote',
   669	  'elided_quote',
   670	  'quote_too_short',
   671	  'quote_does_not_bear_statement',
   672	  'quote_carries_more_than_statement',
   673	  'quote_is_a_fragment',
   674	  'quote_omits_surrounding_text',
   675	  'superseded_by_later_message',
   676	  'statement_is_not_an_assertion',
   677	  'statement_uncheckable',
   678	  'ambiguous_quote',
   679	  'attributed_person_not_author',
   680	] as const;
   681	
   682	export type ProvenanceProblemKind = (typeof PROVENANCE_PROBLEM_KINDS)[number];
   683	
   684	/**
   685	 * What a problem *means*, which is not the same as what it is.
   686	 *
   687	 * The spike's third check reads: "for Claim/Commitment, require the
   688	 * claimant/owner to be the author of at least one cited message, **or force
   689	 * `attribution: third_party`**". That "or" is load-bearing and it splits by
   690	 * type:
   691	 *
   692	 *  - On a **claim**, a claimant who wrote none of the cited messages is a
   693	 *    *wrong receipt*. "X said Y" where X did not say it is the failure mode, and
   694	 *    the reading is discarded.
   695	 *  - On a **commitment**, the same finding is *the ordinary case #4 is about*:
   696	 *    "Justin will handle it" is a real commitment, correctly extracted, that
   697	 *    happens to be third-party. Discarding it would delete exactly the flow #4
   698	 *    spends most of its length designing — the named owner never gets asked.
   699	 *
   700	 * So the finding is reported either way and its consequence is carried with it.
   701	 */
   702	export type ProvenanceSeverity =
   703	  /** Demote below θ_min: the reading is wrong. */
   704	  | 'reject'
   705	  /**
   706	   * **The check cannot judge this, so a person must.** Never auto-accepted, never
   707	   * discarded: the reading stays staged, quietly, with its quote attached.
   708	   *
   709	   * The third severity exists because r4's bearing check has exactly one
   710	   * affirmative answer and two different negative ones, and collapsing them would
   711	   * repeat the mistake it was built to fix. "The statement says words that are not
   712	   * in the quote" is a finding — the receipt points somewhere that does not say
   713	   * this, and the reading is wrong. "The quote says more than the statement" is
   714	   * *not* a finding: the dropped words may be an aside or may be `not`, and no
   715	   * amount of string comparison distinguishes them. Reporting a guess as a verdict
   716	   * in either direction is the class of error this whole file exists to refuse, so
   717	   * the honest output is "I decline, here is the discrepancy, you look".
   718	   */
   719	  | 'refer'
   720	  /** Keep the reading, treat it as third-party attribution. */
   721	  | 'reclassify';
   722	
   723	export interface ProvenanceProblem {
   724	  kind: ProvenanceProblemKind;
   725	  severity: ProvenanceSeverity;
   726	  /** Human-readable, and specific enough to act on. */
   727	  detail: string;
   728	  /** The message involved, when one message is at fault. */
   729	  messageId: string | null;
   730	}
   731	
   732	/** The slice of a proposal these checks read. */
   733	export interface ProvenanceSubject {
   734	  type: AcceptedObjectType;
   735	  provenance: readonly string[];
   736	  quote?: string | null;
   737	  proposer?: { kind: 'model' | 'human' };
   738	  /** `claimant` for a claim, `owner` for a commitment. */
   739	  attributedTo?: string | null;
   740	  /**
   741	   * The sentence being asserted — `statement` / `question` / `title`, whichever
   742	   * the type carries. Supplied so the quote can be checked against *what is
   743	   * being minted* rather than only against the messages.
   744	   *
   745	   * Optional in the type because a human-staged subject has nothing to check.
   746	   * **For a model subject its absence is a refusal, not a skip** — r3 let it be
   747	   * "honest silence", which is the fail-open shape every round of this ticket has
   748	   * found somewhere else: a check that does not run reads identically to a check
   749	   * that passed. A model subject with no statement gets `statement_uncheckable`.
   750	   * Every caller inside this package supplies it.
   751	   */
   752	  statement?: string | null;
   753	}
   754	
   755	/**
   756	 * The three deterministic post-checks the spike specified, plus the two
   757	 * structural ones they imply. Every one of these caught a real error in the
   758	 * spike, and none of them needs a model.
   759	 *
   760	 * A proposal with any problem should be **demoted below θ_min** — discarded,
   761	 * not surfaced (`acceptance.ts` does this when the problems are passed in). The
   762	 * reasoning is the whole point of the receipt: a reading whose citation is
   763	 * wrong is worse than no reading, because the citation is what a person clicks
   764	 * to check it, and a wrong one survives casual review.
   765	 */
   766	export function validateProposalProvenance(
   767	  subject: ProvenanceSubject,
   768	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
   769	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   770	): ProvenanceProblem[] {
   771	  const problems: ProvenanceProblem[] = [];
   772	  const byId = new Map(messages.map((message) => [message.id, message]));
   773	
   774	  // The strictness below is aimed at *machine* readings. A person staging their
   775	  // own reading is the receipt (#4) and is not asked to quote themselves, so the
   776	  // checks that would demand a quote of them are scoped rather than universal.
   777	  const fromModel = subject.proposer?.kind === 'model';
   778	
   779	  if (subject.provenance.length === 0) {
   780	    if (fromModel) {
   781	      problems.push({
   782	        kind: 'no_provenance',
   783	        severity: 'reject',
   784	        detail: 'a model proposal must cite at least one source message',
   785	        messageId: null,
   786	      });
   787	    }
   788	    return problems;
   789	  }
   790	
   791	  const cited: ProvenanceMessage[] = [];
   792	  for (const id of subject.provenance) {
   793	    const message = byId.get(id);
   794	    if (!message) {
   795	      problems.push({
   796	        kind: 'unknown_message',
   797	        severity: 'reject',
   798	        detail: `cites message "${id}", which is not in the window`,
   799	        messageId: id,
   800	      });
   801	      continue;
   802	    }
   803	    cited.push(message);
   804	  }
   805	
   806	  const quote = hasContent(subject.quote) ? (subject.quote as string).trim() : '';
   807	  const normalizedQuote = normalizeForReceipt(quote);
   808	
   809	  // ── A model reading must quote the message it was read out of ────────────
   810	  //
   811	  // `Proposal` already refuses this at parse time. It is checked again here
   812	  // because r2's gauntlet found the parse never happened: the reducer folded
   813	  // whatever object it was handed, so the schema was a manner and not a
   814	  // boundary. Both are fixed — `appendEvent`/`reduce` parse now — and this stays,
   815	  // because "absent or empty required receipt input is a refusal" has to be true
   816	  // in the layer that computes the receipt, not only in the layer above it.
   817	  //
   818	  // **Every model-minted type, since r4.** It used to be scoped to claims and
   819	  // commitments, on the argument that those are the two that put a name on
   820	  // somebody. r3's gauntlet walked through the gap: a model *objective* with
   821	  // `quote: null`, citing one message whose body is `""`, passed every check
   822	  // there was — the citation array was non-empty, so the window looked supplied,
   823	  // and no quote meant no quote check ran. The receipt is not only about
   824	  // attribution. It answers "which sentence, in which message" for every type,
   825	  // and a type with no answer to that has no receipt at all.
   826	  if (fromModel && normalizedQuote.length === 0) {
   827	    problems.push({
   828	      kind: 'missing_quote',
   829	      severity: 'reject',
   830	      detail: `a model ${subject.type} proposal must quote the message that carries it — with no quote nothing identifies which message this was read out of${subject.attributedTo ? ` or which one named "${subject.attributedTo}"` : ''}, and the honest reading of no answer is that nobody did`,
   831	      messageId: null,
   832	    });
   833	  }
   834	
   835	  /** The one cited message whose own words carry the quote, when there is one. */
   836	  let bearing: ProvenanceMessage | null = null;
   837	  /** Several cited authors carry it — nothing can be attributed. */
   838	  let ambiguous = false;
   839	
   840	  if (normalizedQuote.length > 0 && cited.length > 0) {
   841	    const inFullText = cited.filter((message) =>
   842	      normalizeForReceipt(message.body).includes(normalizedQuote),
   843	    );
   844	    const inOwnText = bearingMessages(quote, cited);
   845	
   846	    if (inOwnText.length === 0 && inFullText.length > 0) {
   847	      // The nastiest class: the quote is real, the attribution is real, and the
   848	      // receipt still points at a person who did not say it.
   849	      problems.push({
   850	        kind: 'quote_only_in_reply_blockquote',
   851	        severity: 'reject',
   852	        detail: `the quote appears in message "${inFullText[0]?.id}" only inside a reply-blockquote — its author was quoting someone else, so this citation attributes the words to the wrong person`,
   853	        messageId: inFullText[0]?.id ?? null,
   854	      });
   855	    } else if (inFullText.length === 0) {
   856	      problems.push(
   857	        ELISION.test(quote)
   858	          ? {
   859	              kind: 'elided_quote',
   860	              severity: 'reject',
   861	              detail: `the quote was shortened with an ellipsis and does not appear verbatim in any cited message: "${clip(quote, 80)}"`,
   862	              messageId: null,
   863	            }
   864	          : {
   865	              kind: 'quote_not_found',
   866	              severity: 'reject',
   867	              detail: `the quote appears in none of the cited messages: "${clip(quote, 80)}"`,
   868	              messageId: null,
   869	            },
   870	      );
   871	    }
   872	
   873	    // ── Which message bears it, and is that answerable at all ──────────────
   874	    //
   875	    // r2's gauntlet: "when several cited messages match, refuse as ambiguous
   876	    // rather than picking the first in window order". Window order is an
   877	    // accident of ingestion, and picking an author by accident is exactly the
   878	    // failure the whole receipt exists to prevent.
   879	    //
   880	    // Several matches by *one* author is not that failure — "who wrote this" has
   881	    // a single answer, and refusing a repeated sentence would fire on every
   882	    // thread where somebody says the same thing twice. So the refusal is scoped
   883	    // to the case where the answer is genuinely undetermined: more than one
   884	    // author.
   885	    const authors = new Set(inOwnText.map((message) => message.authorId));
   886	    if (authors.size > 1) {
   887	      ambiguous = true;
   888	      problems.push({
   889	        kind: 'ambiguous_quote',
   890	        severity: 'reject',
   891	        detail: `the quote appears in the own text of ${inOwnText.length} cited messages written by different people (${[
   892	          ...authors,
   893	        ]
   894	          .sort()
   895	          .map((author) => `"${author}"`)
   896	          .join(
   897	            ', ',
   898	          )}) — nothing says which of them said it, and picking the first in window order would attribute it by accident`,
   899	        messageId: null,
   900	      });
   901	    } else {
   902	      bearing = inOwnText[0] ?? null;
   903	    }
   904	
   905	    // ── Long enough to be quoting something ───────────────────────────────
   906	    if (fromModel && normalizedQuote.length < policy.minQuoteLength) {
   907	      problems.push({
   908	        kind: 'quote_too_short',
   909	        severity: 'reject',
   910	        detail: `the quote "${clip(quote, 40)}" is ${normalizedQuote.length} characters, below the ${policy.minQuoteLength} a receipt needs — a span that short occurs in any thread, so it identifies the conversation rather than the sentence`,
   911	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
   912	      });
   913	    }
   914	
   915	    // ── …and cut at the sentence, not wherever the model liked ────────────
   916	    //
   917	    // r4's own blind review. A model that chooses the span can leave the
   918	    // inverter outside it — "It is not true that Bob will deploy production
   919	    // Friday", quoted from "Bob" onwards — and every check in this file, the
   920	    // strict bearing comparison included, passes on the result. See
   921	    // `quoteSpansWholeSentences`. `refer` rather than `reject`: a fragment may be
   922	    // a perfectly fair quotation, and nothing here can read the rest of the
   923	    // sentence to find out, which is the definition of the third severity.
   924	    if (fromModel && bearing) {
   925	      const ownText = stripReplyBlockquotes(bearing.body);
   926	      if (!quoteSpansWholeSentences(quote, ownText, policy)) {
   927	        problems.push({
   928	          kind: 'quote_is_a_fragment',
   929	          severity: 'refer',
   930	          detail: `the quote is a span cut out of the middle of a sentence in message "${bearing.id}" rather than one or more whole sentences of it — the words on either side of the cut may qualify or reverse it ("it is not true that …"), and nothing here can read them, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word`,
   931	          messageId: bearing.id,
   932	        });
   933	      } else if (!quoteCoversOwnText(quote, ownText, policy)) {
   934	        // ── …and the sentences either side of it are not somebody's scissors ──
   935	        //
   936	        // r5, and it is r4's own documented residue turned into a disposition.
   937	        // r4 wrote: *"polarity that lives in a different sentence ('I will deploy
   938	        // Friday. Not.') is not visible to this and is not visible to any span
   939	        // rule"*. True, correct about the mechanism, and the code auto-accepted
   940	        // the sentence anyway — while the same round built the third severity
   941	        // that exactly fits it. A stated limit is not a disposition.
   942	        //
   943	        // The rule is "the quote is everything this author wrote here", not a
   944	        // list of the constructions a neighbour might use, because that list is
   945	        // unbounded in the way `RETRO.md` has now recorded three times. See
   946	        // `quoteCoversOwnText` for the candidates that were tried and what broke
   947	        // each of them.
   948	        problems.push({
   949	          kind: 'quote_omits_surrounding_text',
   950	          severity: 'refer',
   951	          detail: `the quote is whole sentences of message "${bearing.id}" but not all of it — its author wrote more around them, and a neighbouring sentence can reverse, condition or withdraw the one being quoted ("… Not.", "Unless CI is red.", "Correction: …") in a way no rule about the quoted span can see, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word`,
   952	          messageId: bearing.id,
   953	        });
   954	      }
   955	    }
   956	
   957	    // ── …and nobody in the window has taken it back since ──────────────────
   958	    //
   959	    // r5. `ProvenanceMessage` carries `id`, `authorId` and `body`, and until now
   960	    // this function constructed and inspected only the *cited* messages — so a
   961	    // window holding "We will deploy production Friday." followed by
   962	    // "Correction: we will not deploy production Friday.", citing only the
   963	    // first, was a clean receipt. The message table being append-only stops the
   964	    // first message being edited; it does not make the second part of
   965	    // acceptance.
   966	    //
   967	    // Deliberately not a marker list ("correction", "actually", `s/x/y/`) —
   968	    // those route which model reads a window and have no business deciding
   969	    // whether a reading becomes a fact. The test is structural and reuses the
   970	    // alignment: a later message **restates the statement and adds to it**. That
   971	    // catches the inserted `not`, the added `unless`, and the retraction verb,
   972	    // without an opinion about which words are dangerous. An exact restatement
   973	    // is not a correction and a message about something else does not align at
   974	    // all.
   975	    if (fromModel && cited.length > 0) {
   976	      const revisited = laterRevision(
   977	        subject.statement ?? '',
   978	        subject.provenance,
   979	        messages,
   980	        policy,
   981	      );
   982	      if (revisited) {
   983	        problems.push({
   984	          kind: 'superseded_by_later_message',
   985	          severity: 'refer',
   986	          detail: `message "${revisited.message.id}" comes after every message this cites and says the same sentence with ${revisited.added.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')} added — a later message in the same window revisits the one being quoted, and whether that reverses it, narrows it or merely repeats it is not something a machine may decide from the words`,
   987	          messageId: revisited.message.id,
   988	        });
   989	      }
   990	    }
   991	
   992	    // ── …and it is being offered as an assertion at all ────────────────────
   993	    //
   994	    // r5. `objects.ts` requires a nonempty string and the receipt proves string
   995	    // equality, so `"Would we deploy production Friday?"` minted as a `claim`
   996	    // with an identical quote had a perfect receipt and turned somebody's
   997	    // question into their position. `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens` already
   998	    // makes this argument in the other direction — `?` is kept out of the set
   999	    // the bearing check will forgive because "Bob will deploy Friday?" is a
  1000	    // question and minting it as an assertion is the same defect in different
  1001	    // clothes. Here the mark is read rather than compared.
  1002	    if (fromModel && subject.type !== 'open_question' && !isAssertion(subject.statement ?? '')) {
  1003	      problems.push({
  1004	        kind: 'statement_is_not_an_assertion',
  1005	        severity: 'refer',
  1006	        detail: `the ${subject.type} being minted carries a question mark — a question quoted verbatim is an open question or a referral, never an assertion about what somebody holds, and nothing here can tell a rhetorical question from a real one`,
  1007	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1008	      });
  1009	    }
  1010	
  1011	    // ── …and actually carrying the sentence being minted ──────────────────
  1012	    //
  1013	    // The check r2's gauntlet asked for by name and r3's gauntlet found built out
  1014	    // of the wrong instrument. Everything above can pass on a quote that has
  1015	    // nothing to do with the payload — cite the message where Bob wrote "yes,
  1016	    // that works for me", quote it, mint "Bob will deploy on Friday" — and r3's
  1017	    // lexical-overlap answer to that passed something worse: a quote that says
  1018	    // the **opposite** of the payload. See `statementBearing`.
  1019	    if (fromModel) {
  1020	      const statement = subject.statement ?? '';
  1021	      const bearingResult = statementBearing(quote, statement, policy);
  1022	      if (bearingResult.undecidable !== null) {
  1023	        // Not "no problem found" — no check performed. The two are the same
  1024	        // shape in the code and opposite facts about the world, and every finding
  1025	        // in this campaign has been one of them wearing the other's clothes.
  1026	        problems.push({
  1027	          kind: 'statement_uncheckable',
  1028	          severity: 'reject',
  1029	          detail:
  1030	            bearingResult.undecidable === 'empty_statement'
  1031	              ? `the ${subject.type} asserts nothing that can be checked against its quote — with no statement there is no sentence for the receipt to bear, and an unchecked receipt is not a passed one`
  1032	              : bearingResult.undecidable === 'too_long'
  1033	                ? `the quote or the statement is longer than the ${policy.maxAlignedTokens} words this check will align, so it was not checked — an input too large to verify is refused rather than approximated`
  1034	                : 'the quote carries no words at all, so nothing could be checked against the statement',
  1035	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1036	        });
  1037	      } else if (bearingResult.unmatchedInStatement.length > 0) {
  1038	        // The reading asserts words that are not in the record. This is a
  1039	        // verdict, not a hesitation: the citation leads a reader to a sentence
  1040	        // that does not say this.
  1041	        problems.push({
  1042	          kind: 'quote_does_not_bear_statement',
  1043	          severity: 'reject',
  1044	          detail: `"${clip(statement, 60)}" asserts ${bearingResult.unmatchedInStatement.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')}, which the quote does not say — the quoted span is from a cited message but it is not this sentence, so the citation leads a reader somewhere that does not say this`,
  1045	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1046	        });
  1047	      } else if (bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.length > 0) {
  1048	        // Every word of the statement is in the quote, in order, and the quote
  1049	        // says more. `not` is this case. So is `unless CI is red`. So is a
  1050	        // harmless "I think". The check declines rather than guessing.
  1051	        problems.push({
  1052	          kind: 'quote_carries_more_than_statement',
  1053	          severity: 'refer',
  1054	          detail: `the quote says ${bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')} and "${clip(statement, 60)}" drops ${bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.length === 1 ? 'it' : 'them'} — those words may be an aside or may reverse the sentence, and nothing here can tell which, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word; a person has to read the quote`,
  1055	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1056	        });
  1057	      }
  1058	    }
  1059	  }
  1060	
  1061	  // #4's rule, mechanized: nobody gets committed — or quoted — by someone
  1062	  // else's sentence.
  1063	  //
  1064	  // **Bound to the message bearing the sentence, not to the citation list.**
  1065	  // r1's gauntlet: "self-stated if the owner authored *any* cited message —
  1066	  // padding provenance flips attribution". It does. Cite one message the owner
  1067	  // wrote about anything at all, next to the message where somebody else
  1068	  // committed them, and the old check read the pair as a self-statement and
  1069	  // auto-accepted an obligation nobody agreed to. The quote identifies which
  1070	  // message carries the sentence; that message's author is the only one whose
  1071	  // authorship means anything here.
  1072	  //
  1073	  // **The citation-list fallback is gone for machine readings.** r2 kept it on
  1074	  // the argument that it "can only be more likely to report a problem, never
  1075	  // less", and that was wrong in one direction r2's gauntlet found: with an
  1076	  // *empty* quote none of the quote checks above run, no bearing message is
  1077	  // computed, and the fallback reads "somebody in the citation list wrote
  1078	  // something" as support — the padding attack, reopened through the empty
  1079	  // string. A model reading with no determinable bearing message is unsupported,
  1080	  // full stop. A human's still falls back, because a person staging a reading is
  1081	  // not quoting anyone.
  1082	  const attributed = subject.attributedTo;
  1083	  const namesAPerson = subject.type === 'claim' || subject.type === 'commitment';
  1084	  if (attributed && cited.length > 0 && namesAPerson) {
  1085	    const supported = bearing
  1086	      ? bearing.authorId === attributed
  1087	      : fromModel
  1088	        ? false
  1089	        : cited.some((message) => message.authorId === attributed);
  1090	    if (!supported) {
  1091	      const claim = subject.type === 'claim';
  1092	      const where = bearing
  1093	        ? `the message bearing it ("${bearing.id}") was written by "${bearing.authorId}"`
  1094	        : ambiguous
  1095	          ? 'more than one cited author carries the quote, so no message can be said to bear it'
  1096	          : fromModel
  1097	            ? 'no cited message bears the quote, so nothing identifies the message that named them'
  1098	            : `authored none of the cited messages (${cited.map((message) => `"${message.authorId}"`).join(', ')})`;
  1099	      problems.push({
  1100	        kind: 'attributed_person_not_author',
  1101	        // The split the spike's "or force `attribution: third_party`" implies.
  1102	        // A claim whose claimant said nothing is a wrong receipt; a commitment
  1103	        // whose owner said nothing is the third-party case #4 is entirely about,
  1104	        // and discarding it would delete the confirm flow rather than trigger it.
  1105	        severity: claim ? 'reject' : 'reclassify',
  1106	        detail: claim
  1107	          ? `claimant "${attributed}" ${where} — the receipt does not support "X said Y"`
  1108	          : `owner "${attributed}" ${where} — this is third-party attribution, not a self-statement, so it needs their confirm`,
  1109	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1110	      });
  1111	    }
  1112	  }
  1113	
  1114	  return problems;
  1115	}
  1116	
  1117	/**
  1118	 * **Does a message later in this window say the quoted sentence again, with
  1119	 * something added?**
  1120	 *
  1121	 * "Later" is positional: after every message the proposal cites. `EscalationWindow`
  1122	 * documents its messages as being in room order, and the acceptance path is
  1123	 * handed the same window, so position is the ordering the room itself has.
  1124	 * Messages the proposal cites are not later than themselves, and a message
  1125	 * *before* the citations is not a revision of them.
  1126	 *
  1127	 * The comparison runs over **routing** tokens rather than receipt tokens, and
  1128	 * that is the one place on the receipt path where the lossy fold is correct: this
  1129	 * is a detector, not a certification. A false positive costs a referral — a
  1130	 * person reads two sentences — and a false negative costs an auto-accepted
  1131	 * statement the room has already walked back. Case, fullwidth spellings and
  1132	 * markdown are all noise to that question.
  1133	 *
  1134	 * Returns the tokens the later message added, because a refusal that does not
  1135	 * say what changed is a dead end.
  1136	 */
  1137	export function laterRevision(
  1138	  statement: string,
  1139	  citedIds: readonly string[],
  1140	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1141	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
  1142	): { message: ProvenanceMessage; added: string[] } | null {
  1143	  if (isBlank(statement)) return null;
  1144	  const wanted = routingTokens(statement);
  1145	  if (wanted.length === 0 || wanted.length > policy.maxAlignedTokens) return null;
  1146	
  1147	  const cited = new Set(citedIds);
  1148	  let lastCited = -1;
  1149	  for (const [index, message] of messages.entries()) {
  1150	    if (cited.has(message.id)) lastCited = index;
  1151	  }
  1152	  if (lastCited === -1) return null;
  1153	
  1154	  const later = messages.slice(lastCited + 1, lastCited + 1 + policy.maxLaterMessagesScanned);
  1155	  for (const message of later) {
  1156	    const own = stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body);
  1157	    const sentences = sentencesOf(own);
  1158	    if (sentences.length > policy.maxScannedSentences) continue;
  1159	    for (const sentence of sentences) {
  1160	      const aligned = alignTokens(routingTokens(sentence), wanted, policy);
  1161	      if (aligned.undecidable !== null) continue;
  1162	      // Every word of the statement is here, in order, and this sentence says
  1163	      // more. An exact restatement (`unmatchedInQuote` empty) is agreement, and
  1164	      // a sentence missing any of the statement's words is about something else.
  1165	      if (aligned.unmatchedInStatement.length === 0 && aligned.unmatchedInQuote.length > 0) {
  1166	        return { message, added: aligned.unmatchedInQuote };
  1167	      }
  1168	    }
  1169	  }
  1170	  return null;
  1171	}
  1172	
  1173	/**
  1174	 * Every cited message whose *own* text carries a quoted span — reply-blockquotes
  1175	 * stripped, so a message that merely quoted the sentence back does not count.
  1176	 *
  1177	 * Returns all of them rather than the first, because "how many" is the question
  1178	 * `ambiguous_quote` asks and a function that returns one answer cannot be asked
  1179	 * it.
  1180	 */
  1181	export function bearingMessages(
  1182	  quote: string,
  1183	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1184	): ProvenanceMessage[] {
  1185	  const normalized = normalizeForReceipt(quote);
  1186	  if (normalized.length === 0) return [];
  1187	  return messages.filter((message) =>
  1188	    normalizeForReceipt(stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body)).includes(normalized),
  1189	  );
  1190	}
  1191	
  1192	/**
  1193	 * The cited message that actually carries a quoted span, when exactly one person
  1194	 * can be said to have written it.
  1195	 *
  1196	 * This is the function that makes attribution answerable. "Did the owner say
  1197	 * this?" is a question about one message, and every version of it that ranges
  1198	 * over the citation list instead is answerable by padding the list.
  1199	 *
  1200	 * `null` covers three cases that all mean the same thing here — no cited message
  1201	 * carries it, the quote is empty, or several *different authors* carry it. All
  1202	 * three leave "who said this" undetermined, and an undetermined author is not a
  1203	 * cheaper kind of answer: `commitmentAttribution` reads `null` as third-party and
  1204	 * `validateProposalProvenance` reports it.
  1205	 */
  1206	export function bearingMessage(
  1207	  quote: string,
  1208	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1209	): ProvenanceMessage | null {
  1210	  const borne = bearingMessages(quote, messages);
  1211	  if (borne.length === 0) return null;
  1212	  const authors = new Set(borne.map((message) => message.authorId));
  1213	  return authors.size === 1 ? (borne[0] ?? null) : null;
  1214	}
  1215	
  1216	/** True when nothing is wrong with the receipt. */
  1217	export function provenanceIsClean(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): boolean {
  1218	  return problems.length === 0;
  1219	}
  1220	
  1221	/** The problems that mean "throw this reading away". */
  1222	export function rejectingProblems(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): ProvenanceProblem[] {
  1223	  return problems.filter((problem) => problem.severity === 'reject');
  1224	}
  1225	
  1226	/** The problems that mean "a person has to look at this before it counts". */
  1227	export function referringProblems(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): ProvenanceProblem[] {
  1228	  return problems.filter((problem) => problem.severity === 'refer');
  1229	}
  1230	
  1231	/**
  1232	 * Every problem that stops a machine accepting the reading — `reject` **and**
  1233	 * `refer`, and not `reclassify`, which is a route rather than a refusal.
  1234	 *
  1235	 * This exists so a caller cannot add a severity and quietly fail open by
  1236	 * filtering for the one it remembers. Anything that is not explicitly a route is
  1237	 * a block.
  1238	 */
  1239	export function blockingProblems(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): ProvenanceProblem[] {
  1240	  return problems.filter((problem) => problem.severity !== 'reclassify');
  1241	}

     5	  type ProvenanceProblem,
     6	  referringProblems,
     7	  rejectingProblems,
     8	  validateProposalProvenance,
     9	} from './escalation.js';
    10	import { type AuthoredEvent, authored, type CoreEvent, trustedContext } from './events.js';
    11	import { alignTokens, hasContent, routingTokens } from './matching.js';
    12	import {
    13	  type AcceptedObjectType,
    14	  type ClaimPayload,
    15	  type DecisionPayload,
    16	  payloadText,
    17	} from './objects.js';
    18	import { type AcceptanceConfig, defaultAcceptanceConfig } from './policy.js';
    19	import type { Proposal, StoredProposal } from './proposal.js';
