   350	 *     Absent, empty, and empty-of-content are the same fact about the world.
   351	 *  4. **A quote at all**, for every model-minted type. The schema requires it;
   352	 *     this re-requires it, because round 2's gauntlet found the schema was never
   353	 *     run on the fold path. An empty quote is the same absence as a missing one,
   354	 *     and it is the input the bearing and attribution rules are computed from.
   355	 *  5. **The receipt itself** — the quote is long enough to identify a sentence,
   356	 *     it is in a cited message, it is that author's own text rather than
   357	 *     something they were quoting, exactly one author carries it, and the
   358	 *     statement being minted is that quote with nothing dropped but articles.
   359	 *  6. **Certifiability.** A quote that says *more* than the statement is not
   360	 *     refused as wrong and is not accepted as right: nothing here can tell an
   360	 *     refused as wrong and is not accepted as right: nothing here can tell an
   361	 *     aside from a "not", so a machine may not act on it and a person must.
   362	 *  7. **Third-party attribution.** A commitment whose owner did not write the
   363	 *     message bearing it is not a self-statement, and #4 is unambiguous that
   364	 *     nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence. It is a real reading and
   365	 *     it goes to the named person to confirm — through a human acceptance, not
   366	 *     through this path.
   367	 *
   368	 * A human acceptance runs none of this: a person accepting a reading has read
   369	 * it, and their judgement is the receipt. That asymmetry is the product.
   370	 */
   371	export function acceptanceReceiptRefusal(input: {
   372	  actor: Actor;
   373	  proposalId: string;
   374	  proposal: StoredProposal;
   375	  object: AcceptedObject;
   376	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[] | undefined;
   377	}): AcceptanceReceiptRefusal | null {
   378	  const { object, proposal, proposalId } = input;
   379	  const who = actorName(input.actor);
   380	
   381	  if (canonicalJson(object.payload) !== canonicalJson(proposal.payload)) {
   382	    return {
   383	      gate: 'payload_binding',
   384	      detail: `object "${object.id}" cites proposal "${proposalId}" but does not carry its payload — ${who} may only accept the reading that was staged, not mint a different one behind a citation that says a person could have checked it`,
   385	    };
   386	  }
   387	
   388	  const objectCites = [...object.provenance.messageIds].sort();
   389	  const proposalCites = [...proposal.provenance].sort();
   390	  if (canonicalJson(objectCites) !== canonicalJson(proposalCites)) {
   391	    return {
   392	      gate: 'provenance_binding',
   393	      detail: `object "${object.id}" cites messages [${objectCites.join(', ')}] but proposal "${proposalId}" was staged against [${proposalCites.join(', ')}] — the receipt may not change on the way through acceptance; it is what the attribution rules are computed over`,
   394	    };
   395	  }
   396	
   397	  // ── A window at all, and emptiness as a property of the content ───────────
   398	  //
   399	  // Round 2 closed `undefined`; round 2's gauntlet reopened it with `[]`; round
   400	  // 3's gauntlet reopened it again with `[{ id, authorId, body: '' }]` — one
   401	  // message, so `length === 0` is false, and nothing in it. All three are the
   402	  // same fact: nobody supplied the messages this reading cites. The test is
   403	  // therefore about content, not about the array, and `hasContent` is the one
   404	  // place that decides what content is (a letter or a digit — not a list of the
   405	  // invisible characters somebody has thought of).
   406	  const window = input.messages;
   407	  if (window === undefined || window.length === 0 || !window.some((m) => hasContent(m.body))) {
   408	    const how =
   409	      window === undefined
   410	        ? 'no message window supplied'
   411	        : window.length === 0
   412	          ? 'an empty message window supplied'
   413	          : `a message window of ${window.length} message${window.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} with nothing in any of their bodies`;
   414	    return {
   415	      gate: 'missing_receipt_context',
   416	      detail: `${who} accepted proposal "${proposalId}" with ${how}, so its receipt could not be checked — a reading whose citation cannot be verified is refused, never accepted on trust; a window with no words in it is not a window, it is the same absence written differently`,
   417	    };
   418	  }
   419	
   420	  // ── A quote at all, for every model-minted type ───────────────────────────
   421	  //
   422	  // Scoped to claims and commitments until r4, on the argument that they are the
   423	  // two that put a name on somebody. That is true and it is not the whole job:
   424	  // r3's gauntlet minted a model *objective* with `quote: null` against a window
   425	  // of empty bodies, and no quote-length, bearing or attribution check ran on it
   426	  // at all. The quote is what answers "which sentence, in which message" — every
   427	  // type needs that answer, and a type with no quote has no receipt to check.
   428	  if (isBlank(proposal.quote)) {
   429	    return {
   430	      gate: 'missing_quote',
   431	      detail: `${who} accepted ${object.type} proposal "${proposalId}", which quotes nothing — a receipt names the sentence it rests on and only the quote identifies it, so a model-minted ${object.type} with an absent or empty quote is refused rather than resting on whoever happens to be in the citation list`,
   432	    };
   433	  }
   434	
   435	  const attributedTo =
   436	    object.type === 'claim'
   437	      ? object.payload.claimant
   438	      : object.type === 'commitment'
   439	        ? object.payload.owner
   440	        : null;
   441	
   442	  const problems = validateProposalProvenance(
   443	    {
   444	      type: proposal.type,
   445	      provenance: proposal.provenance,
   446	      quote: proposal.quote,
   447	      proposer: proposal.proposer,
   448	      attributedTo,
   449	      // The sentence the object actually asserts — checked *from the object*
   450	      // rather than from the proposal on purpose. Payload binding above has
   451	      // already proved the two are identical, so reading it here says what this
   452	      // check is about: the quote has to bear the thing being minted.
   453	      statement: objectStatement(object),
   454	    },
   455	    window,
   456	  );
   457	
   458	  const rejecting = rejectingProblems(problems);
   459	  if (rejecting.length > 0) {
   460	    return {
   461	      gate: 'receipt_failed',
   462	      detail: `${who} accepted proposal "${proposalId}" on a receipt that does not hold: ${rejecting.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   463	    };
   464	  }
   465	
   466	  // A receipt the check declines to rule on is not a receipt that passed. The
   467	  // acceptance is refused with its own gate name so the log distinguishes "the
   468	  // citation is wrong" from "the citation may be right and nothing here can say
   469	  // so" — the second one is a question for a person, and a person accepting it
   470	  // runs none of this.
   471	  const referring = referringProblems(problems);
   472	  if (referring.length > 0) {
   473	    return {
   474	      gate: 'receipt_not_certifiable',
   475	      detail: `${who} accepted proposal "${proposalId}" on a receipt this check declines to rule on: ${referring.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   476	    };
   477	  }
   478	
   479	  const thirdParty = problems.find((problem) => problem.kind === 'attributed_person_not_author');
   480	  if (thirdParty) {
   481	    return {
   482	      gate: 'third_party_confirm',
   483	      detail: `${who} accepted proposal "${proposalId}" as a commitment for somebody who did not write it: ${thirdParty.detail}. Nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence (#4) — it waits for the named owner to confirm, and only a human acceptance can carry that confirmation`,
   484	    };
   485	  }
   486	
   487	  return null;
   488	}

   900	      });
   901	    } else {
   902	      bearing = inOwnText[0] ?? null;
   903	    }
   904	
   905	    // ── Long enough to be quoting something ───────────────────────────────
   906	    if (fromModel && normalizedQuote.length < policy.minQuoteLength) {
   907	      problems.push({
   908	        kind: 'quote_too_short',
   909	        severity: 'reject',
   910	        detail: `the quote "${clip(quote, 40)}" is ${normalizedQuote.length} characters, below the ${policy.minQuoteLength} a receipt needs — a span that short occurs in any thread, so it identifies the conversation rather than the sentence`,
   911	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
   912	      });
   913	    }
   914	
   915	    // ── …and cut at the sentence, not wherever the model liked ────────────
   916	    //
   917	    // r4's own blind review. A model that chooses the span can leave the
   918	    // inverter outside it — "It is not true that Bob will deploy production
   919	    // Friday", quoted from "Bob" onwards — and every check in this file, the
   920	    // strict bearing comparison included, passes on the result. See
   921	    // `quoteSpansWholeSentences`. `refer` rather than `reject`: a fragment may be
   922	    // a perfectly fair quotation, and nothing here can read the rest of the
   923	    // sentence to find out, which is the definition of the third severity.
   924	    if (fromModel && bearing) {
   925	      const ownText = stripReplyBlockquotes(bearing.body);
   926	      if (!quoteSpansWholeSentences(quote, ownText, policy)) {
   927	        problems.push({
   928	          kind: 'quote_is_a_fragment',
   929	          severity: 'refer',
   930	          detail: `the quote is a span cut out of the middle of a sentence in message "${bearing.id}" rather than one or more whole sentences of it — the words on either side of the cut may qualify or reverse it ("it is not true that …"), and nothing here can read them, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word`,
   931	          messageId: bearing.id,
   932	        });
   933	      } else if (!quoteCoversOwnText(quote, ownText, policy)) {
   934	        // ── …and the sentences either side of it are not somebody's scissors ──
   935	        //
   936	        // r5, and it is r4's own documented residue turned into a disposition.
   937	        // r4 wrote: *"polarity that lives in a different sentence ('I will deploy
   938	        // Friday. Not.') is not visible to this and is not visible to any span
   939	        // rule"*. True, correct about the mechanism, and the code auto-accepted
   940	        // the sentence anyway — while the same round built the third severity
   941	        // that exactly fits it. A stated limit is not a disposition.
   942	        //
   943	        // The rule is "the quote is everything this author wrote here", not a
   944	        // list of the constructions a neighbour might use, because that list is
   945	        // unbounded in the way `RETRO.md` has now recorded three times. See
   946	        // `quoteCoversOwnText` for the candidates that were tried and what broke
   947	        // each of them.
   948	        problems.push({
   949	          kind: 'quote_omits_surrounding_text',
   950	          severity: 'refer',
   951	          detail: `the quote is whole sentences of message "${bearing.id}" but not all of it — its author wrote more around them, and a neighbouring sentence can reverse, condition or withdraw the one being quoted ("… Not.", "Unless CI is red.", "Correction: …") in a way no rule about the quoted span can see, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word`,
   952	          messageId: bearing.id,
   953	        });
   954	      }
   955	    }
   956	
   957	    // ── …and nobody in the window has taken it back since ──────────────────
   958	    //
   959	    // r5. `ProvenanceMessage` carries `id`, `authorId` and `body`, and until now
   960	    // this function constructed and inspected only the *cited* messages — so a
   961	    // window holding "We will deploy production Friday." followed by
   962	    // "Correction: we will not deploy production Friday.", citing only the
   963	    // first, was a clean receipt. The message table being append-only stops the
   964	    // first message being edited; it does not make the second part of
   965	    // acceptance.
   966	    //
   967	    // Deliberately not a marker list ("correction", "actually", `s/x/y/`) —
   968	    // those route which model reads a window and have no business deciding
   969	    // whether a reading becomes a fact. The test is structural and reuses the
   970	    // alignment: a later message **restates the statement and adds to it**. That
   971	    // catches the inserted `not`, the added `unless`, and the retraction verb,
   972	    // without an opinion about which words are dangerous. An exact restatement
   973	    // is not a correction and a message about something else does not align at
   974	    // all.
   975	    if (fromModel && cited.length > 0) {
   976	      const revisited = laterRevision(
   977	        subject.statement ?? '',
   978	        subject.provenance,
   979	        messages,
   980	        policy,
   981	      );
   982	      if (revisited) {
   983	        problems.push({
   984	          kind: 'superseded_by_later_message',
   985	          severity: 'refer',
   986	          detail: `message "${revisited.message.id}" comes after every message this cites and says the same sentence with ${revisited.added.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')} added — a later message in the same window revisits the one being quoted, and whether that reverses it, narrows it or merely repeats it is not something a machine may decide from the words`,
   987	          messageId: revisited.message.id,
   988	        });
   989	      }
   990	    }
   991	
   992	    // ── …and it is being offered as an assertion at all ────────────────────
   993	    //
   994	    // r5. `objects.ts` requires a nonempty string and the receipt proves string
   995	    // equality, so `"Would we deploy production Friday?"` minted as a `claim`
   996	    // with an identical quote had a perfect receipt and turned somebody's
   997	    // question into their position. `RECEIPT_POLICY.droppableTokens` already
   998	    // makes this argument in the other direction — `?` is kept out of the set
   999	    // the bearing check will forgive because "Bob will deploy Friday?" is a
  1000	    // question and minting it as an assertion is the same defect in different
  1001	    // clothes. Here the mark is read rather than compared.
  1002	    if (fromModel && subject.type !== 'open_question' && !isAssertion(subject.statement ?? '')) {
  1003	      problems.push({
  1004	        kind: 'statement_is_not_an_assertion',
  1005	        severity: 'refer',
  1006	        detail: `the ${subject.type} being minted carries a question mark — a question quoted verbatim is an open question or a referral, never an assertion about what somebody holds, and nothing here can tell a rhetorical question from a real one`,
  1007	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1008	      });
  1009	    }
  1010	
  1011	    // ── …and actually carrying the sentence being minted ──────────────────
  1012	    //
  1013	    // The check r2's gauntlet asked for by name and r3's gauntlet found built out
  1014	    // of the wrong instrument. Everything above can pass on a quote that has
  1015	    // nothing to do with the payload — cite the message where Bob wrote "yes,
  1016	    // that works for me", quote it, mint "Bob will deploy on Friday" — and r3's
  1017	    // lexical-overlap answer to that passed something worse: a quote that says
  1018	    // the **opposite** of the payload. See `statementBearing`.
  1019	    if (fromModel) {
  1020	      const statement = subject.statement ?? '';
  1021	      const bearingResult = statementBearing(quote, statement, policy);
  1022	      if (bearingResult.undecidable !== null) {
  1023	        // Not "no problem found" — no check performed. The two are the same
  1024	        // shape in the code and opposite facts about the world, and every finding
  1025	        // in this campaign has been one of them wearing the other's clothes.
  1026	        problems.push({
  1027	          kind: 'statement_uncheckable',
  1028	          severity: 'reject',
  1029	          detail:
  1030	            bearingResult.undecidable === 'empty_statement'
  1031	              ? `the ${subject.type} asserts nothing that can be checked against its quote — with no statement there is no sentence for the receipt to bear, and an unchecked receipt is not a passed one`
  1032	              : bearingResult.undecidable === 'too_long'
  1033	                ? `the quote or the statement is longer than the ${policy.maxAlignedTokens} words this check will align, so it was not checked — an input too large to verify is refused rather than approximated`
  1034	                : 'the quote carries no words at all, so nothing could be checked against the statement',
  1035	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1036	        });
  1037	      } else if (bearingResult.unmatchedInStatement.length > 0) {
  1038	        // The reading asserts words that are not in the record. This is a
  1039	        // verdict, not a hesitation: the citation leads a reader to a sentence
  1040	        // that does not say this.
  1041	        problems.push({
  1042	          kind: 'quote_does_not_bear_statement',
  1043	          severity: 'reject',
  1044	          detail: `"${clip(statement, 60)}" asserts ${bearingResult.unmatchedInStatement.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')}, which the quote does not say — the quoted span is from a cited message but it is not this sentence, so the citation leads a reader somewhere that does not say this`,
  1045	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1046	        });
  1047	      } else if (bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.length > 0) {
  1048	        // Every word of the statement is in the quote, in order, and the quote
  1049	        // says more. `not` is this case. So is `unless CI is red`. So is a
  1050	        // harmless "I think". The check declines rather than guessing.
  1051	        problems.push({
  1052	          kind: 'quote_carries_more_than_statement',
  1053	          severity: 'refer',
  1054	          detail: `the quote says ${bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')} and "${clip(statement, 60)}" drops ${bearingResult.unmatchedInQuote.length === 1 ? 'it' : 'them'} — those words may be an aside or may reverse the sentence, and nothing here can tell which, so this reading is not accepted on a machine's word; a person has to read the quote`,
  1055	          messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1056	        });
  1057	      }
  1058	    }
  1059	  }
  1060	
  1061	  // #4's rule, mechanized: nobody gets committed — or quoted — by someone
  1062	  // else's sentence.
  1063	  //
  1064	  // **Bound to the message bearing the sentence, not to the citation list.**
  1065	  // r1's gauntlet: "self-stated if the owner authored *any* cited message —
  1066	  // padding provenance flips attribution". It does. Cite one message the owner
  1067	  // wrote about anything at all, next to the message where somebody else
  1068	  // committed them, and the old check read the pair as a self-statement and
  1069	  // auto-accepted an obligation nobody agreed to. The quote identifies which
  1070	  // message carries the sentence; that message's author is the only one whose
  1071	  // authorship means anything here.
  1072	  //
  1073	  // **The citation-list fallback is gone for machine readings.** r2 kept it on
  1074	  // the argument that it "can only be more likely to report a problem, never
  1075	  // less", and that was wrong in one direction r2's gauntlet found: with an
  1076	  // *empty* quote none of the quote checks above run, no bearing message is
  1077	  // computed, and the fallback reads "somebody in the citation list wrote
  1078	  // something" as support — the padding attack, reopened through the empty
  1079	  // string. A model reading with no determinable bearing message is unsupported,
  1080	  // full stop. A human's still falls back, because a person staging a reading is
  1081	  // not quoting anyone.
  1082	  const attributed = subject.attributedTo;
  1083	  const namesAPerson = subject.type === 'claim' || subject.type === 'commitment';
  1084	  if (attributed && cited.length > 0 && namesAPerson) {
  1085	    const supported = bearing
  1086	      ? bearing.authorId === attributed
  1087	      : fromModel
  1088	        ? false
  1089	        : cited.some((message) => message.authorId === attributed);
  1090	    if (!supported) {
  1091	      const claim = subject.type === 'claim';
  1092	      const where = bearing
  1093	        ? `the message bearing it ("${bearing.id}") was written by "${bearing.authorId}"`
  1094	        : ambiguous
  1095	          ? 'more than one cited author carries the quote, so no message can be said to bear it'
  1096	          : fromModel
  1097	            ? 'no cited message bears the quote, so nothing identifies the message that named them'
  1098	            : `authored none of the cited messages (${cited.map((message) => `"${message.authorId}"`).join(', ')})`;
  1099	      problems.push({
  1100	        kind: 'attributed_person_not_author',
  1101	        // The split the spike's "or force `attribution: third_party`" implies.
  1102	        // A claim whose claimant said nothing is a wrong receipt; a commitment
  1103	        // whose owner said nothing is the third-party case #4 is entirely about,
  1104	        // and discarding it would delete the confirm flow rather than trigger it.
  1105	        severity: claim ? 'reject' : 'reclassify',
  1106	        detail: claim
  1107	          ? `claimant "${attributed}" ${where} — the receipt does not support "X said Y"`
  1108	          : `owner "${attributed}" ${where} — this is third-party attribution, not a self-statement, so it needs their confirm`,
  1109	        messageId: bearing?.id ?? null,
  1110	      });
  1111	    }
  1112	  }
  1113	
  1114	  return problems;
  1115	}
  1116	
  1117	/**
  1118	 * **Does a message later in this window say the quoted sentence again, with
  1119	 * something added?**
  1120	 *
  1121	 * "Later" is positional: after every message the proposal cites. `EscalationWindow`
  1122	 * documents its messages as being in room order, and the acceptance path is
  1123	 * handed the same window, so position is the ordering the room itself has.
  1124	 * Messages the proposal cites are not later than themselves, and a message
  1125	 * *before* the citations is not a revision of them.
  1126	 *
  1127	 * The comparison runs over **routing** tokens rather than receipt tokens, and
  1128	 * that is the one place on the receipt path where the lossy fold is correct: this
  1129	 * is a detector, not a certification. A false positive costs a referral — a
  1130	 * person reads two sentences — and a false negative costs an auto-accepted
  1131	 * statement the room has already walked back. Case, fullwidth spellings and
  1132	 * markdown are all noise to that question.
  1133	 *
  1134	 * Returns the tokens the later message added, because a refusal that does not
  1135	 * say what changed is a dead end.
  1136	 */
  1137	export function laterRevision(
  1138	  statement: string,
  1139	  citedIds: readonly string[],
  1140	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1141	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
  1142	): { message: ProvenanceMessage; added: string[] } | null {
  1143	  if (isBlank(statement)) return null;
  1144	  const wanted = routingTokens(statement);
  1145	  if (wanted.length === 0 || wanted.length > policy.maxAlignedTokens) return null;
  1146	
  1147	  const cited = new Set(citedIds);
  1148	  let lastCited = -1;
  1149	  for (const [index, message] of messages.entries()) {
  1150	    if (cited.has(message.id)) lastCited = index;
  1151	  }
  1152	  if (lastCited === -1) return null;
  1153	
  1154	  const later = messages.slice(lastCited + 1, lastCited + 1 + policy.maxLaterMessagesScanned);
  1155	  for (const message of later) {
  1156	    const own = stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body);
  1157	    const sentences = sentencesOf(own);
  1158	    if (sentences.length > policy.maxScannedSentences) continue;
  1159	    for (const sentence of sentences) {
  1160	      const aligned = alignTokens(routingTokens(sentence), wanted, policy);
  1161	      if (aligned.undecidable !== null) continue;
  1162	      // Every word of the statement is here, in order, and this sentence says
  1163	      // more. An exact restatement (`unmatchedInQuote` empty) is agreement, and
  1164	      // a sentence missing any of the statement's words is about something else.
  1165	      if (aligned.unmatchedInStatement.length === 0 && aligned.unmatchedInQuote.length > 0) {
  1166	        return { message, added: aligned.unmatchedInQuote };
  1167	      }
  1168	    }
  1169	  }
  1170	  return null;
  1171	}
  1172	
  1173	/**
  1174	 * Every cited message whose *own* text carries a quoted span — reply-blockquotes
  1175	 * stripped, so a message that merely quoted the sentence back does not count.
  1176	 *
  1177	 * Returns all of them rather than the first, because "how many" is the question
  1178	 * `ambiguous_quote` asks and a function that returns one answer cannot be asked
  1179	 * it.
  1180	 */
  1181	export function bearingMessages(
  1182	  quote: string,
  1183	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1184	): ProvenanceMessage[] {
  1185	  const normalized = normalizeForReceipt(quote);
  1186	  if (normalized.length === 0) return [];
  1187	  return messages.filter((message) =>
  1188	    normalizeForReceipt(stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body)).includes(normalized),
  1189	  );
  1190	}
  1191	
  1192	/**
  1193	 * The cited message that actually carries a quoted span, when exactly one person
  1194	 * can be said to have written it.
  1195	 *
  1196	 * This is the function that makes attribution answerable. "Did the owner say
  1197	 * this?" is a question about one message, and every version of it that ranges
  1198	 * over the citation list instead is answerable by padding the list.
  1199	 *
  1200	 * `null` covers three cases that all mean the same thing here — no cited message
  1201	 * carries it, the quote is empty, or several *different authors* carry it. All
  1202	 * three leave "who said this" undetermined, and an undetermined author is not a
  1203	 * cheaper kind of answer: `commitmentAttribution` reads `null` as third-party and
  1204	 * `validateProposalProvenance` reports it.
  1205	 */
  1206	export function bearingMessage(
  1207	  quote: string,
  1208	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1209	): ProvenanceMessage | null {
  1210	  const borne = bearingMessages(quote, messages);
  1211	  if (borne.length === 0) return null;
  1212	  const authors = new Set(borne.map((message) => message.authorId));
  1213	  return authors.size === 1 ? (borne[0] ?? null) : null;
  1214	}
  1215	
  1216	/** True when nothing is wrong with the receipt. */
  1217	export function provenanceIsClean(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): boolean {
  1218	  return problems.length === 0;
  1219	}
  1220	
  1221	/** The problems that mean "throw this reading away". */
  1222	export function rejectingProblems(problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[]): ProvenanceProblem[] {
  1223	  return problems.filter((problem) => problem.severity === 'reject');
  1224	}
  1225	
   290	 * side of this trade; the expensive side deleted a contradiction in silence.
   291	 *
   292	 * Routing tokens rather than receipt tokens, deliberately: case, fullwidth
   293	 * spellings and markdown are noise to "is this the same sentence again", and
   294	 * being lossy *here* means firing more often, which for the receipt path is the
   295	 * dangerous direction and for a re-proposal is the harmless one — a text that
   296	 * differs only in case is the same reading twice.
   297	 */
   298	export function findDuplicate(
   299	  type: AcceptedObjectType,
   300	  text: string,
   301	  messageIds: readonly Id[],
   302	  accepted: readonly AcceptedObjectRef[],
   303	): AcceptedObjectRef | null {
   304	  const wanted = routingTokens(text);
   305	  if (wanted.length === 0) return null;
   306	  const cited = new Set(messageIds);
   307	  for (const candidate of accepted) {
   308	    if (candidate.type !== type) continue;
   309	    if (!candidate.messageIds.some((id) => cited.has(id))) continue;
   310	    const have = routingTokens(candidate.text);
   311	    if (have.length === 0) continue;
   312	    // Symmetric by construction: `borne` requires both sides accounted for, so
   313	    // neither "the new one restates the old" nor the reverse is enough alone.
   314	    if (alignTokens(have, wanted).borne) return candidate;
   315	  }
   316	  return null;
   317	}
   318	
   319	/**
   320	 * #4's matrix, applied to one staged reading.
   321	 *
   322	 * The table this implements, exactly — one row per cell, and
   323	 * `acceptance.test.ts` has one test per row:
   324	 *
   325	 * | type                     | c < θ_min | θ_min ≤ c < θ_auto | c ≥ θ_auto            |
   326	 * | ------------------------ | --------- | ------------------ | --------------------- |
   327	 * | claim                    | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   328	 * | open_question            | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   329	 * | objective                | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   330	 * | commitment, self-stated  | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   331	 * | commitment, third-party  | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, owner confirm|
   332	 * | decision                 | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, Needs-you    |
   333	 *
   334	 * …with one cell in front of all of them: **no window, no verdict**. A model
   335	 * proposal judged without the messages it cites is discarded, because the
   336	 * alternative — an empty problem set read as a clean receipt — is how a wrong
   337	 * citation becomes an accepted fact.
   338	 *
   339	 * …and one cell across all of them, above θ_min: **a receipt nothing can rule
   340	 * on is pending, quiet**. Not accepted, because a machine may not act on a
   341	 * quote it cannot certify; not discarded, because the discrepancy is exactly
   342	 * what a person needs to see. `receipt_not_certifiable`.
   343	 *
   344	 * Two cells are worth defending.
   345	 *
   346	 * **Decision at c ≥ θ_auto is Needs-you, not quiet.** #4 says a pending
   347	 * proposal shows quietly "never in Needs-you unless it blocks something", and #6
   348	 * says `needs_decision` is "a decision proposal awaiting you". Both are right,
   349	 * and the θ band is where they meet: a decision the pass is unsure about shows
   350	 * quietly, and one it is confident about is exactly the thing a person should be
   351	 * asked to confirm. A confident decision proposal *does* block something — the
   352	 * room's current state is wrong until somebody rules on it.
   353	 *
   354	 * **Third-party commitment never auto-accepts, at any confidence.** Not a
   355	 * threshold: a rule. The spike could not test it (one commitment in six runs,
   356	 * self-attributed, on a public RFC thread where nobody says "Ryan will handle
   357	 * it"), so it is implemented from #4's text and is the part of this file most
   358	 * in need of the working-team corpus #24 asks for.
   359	 */
   360	export function decideAcceptance(
   361	  proposal: Proposal | StoredProposal,
   362	  context: AcceptanceContext,
   363	): AcceptanceDecision {
   364	  const config = context.config ?? defaultAcceptanceConfig;
   365	  const rule = config[proposal.type];
   366	  const payload = proposal.payload as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
   367	  const text = payloadText(proposal.type, payload);
   368	  const attributedTo = payloadAttributedTo(proposal.type, payload);
   369	  const messages = context.messages as readonly ProvenanceMessage[] | undefined;
   370	
   371	  const base = {
   372	    thetaAuto: rule.thetaAuto,
   373	    thetaMin: rule.thetaMin,
   374	    autoAcceptAvailable: rule.autoAccept,
   375	    attribution: null,
   376	    duplicateOf: null,
   377	    awaitingConfirmFrom: null,
   378	  } as const;
   379	
   380	  // ── No window, no verdict ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   381	  //
   382	  // Fail-closed, and loudly: this is a caller bug, not a reading defect. The
   383	  // failure it replaces was silent in the other direction.
   384	  //
   385	  // **Empty counts as absent.** Round 2's gauntlet found the `undefined`-only
   386	  // form of this check: `messages: []` walked past the door marked "required",
   387	  // and everything downstream then found nothing wrong because there was nothing
   388	  // to look in. The two spellings describe the same state of the world — nobody
   389	  // supplied the messages this reading cites — so they get the same answer.
   390	  //
   391	  // **And empty of content counts as empty.** Round 3's gauntlet found the
   392	  // `length`-only form: one message whose `body` is `""` makes the array
   393	  // non-empty and supplies nothing, which is the same trick one level in. The
   394	  // question is whether anybody supplied words, so the test is `hasContent`.
   395	  const windowIsAbsent =
   396	    messages === undefined || messages.length === 0 || !messages.some((m) => hasContent(m.body));
   397	  if (proposal.proposer.kind === 'model' && windowIsAbsent) {
   398	    return {
   399	      ...base,
   400	      verdict: 'discard',
   401	      visibility: 'none',
   402	      rule: 'missing_message_context',
   403	      reason:
   404	        messages === undefined
   405	          ? 'no message window was supplied, so the receipt could not be checked — a model reading is never accepted on trust; supply the messages it cites'
   406	          : messages.length === 0
   407	            ? 'an empty message window was supplied, so the receipt could not be checked — an empty window is not a window; a model reading is never accepted on trust'
   408	            : `a message window of ${messages.length} message${messages.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} was supplied and not one of them has any words in it, so the receipt could not be checked — a window with nothing in it is not a window; a model reading is never accepted on trust`,
   409	    };
   410	  }
   411	
   412	  // ── The receipt, before anything else ────────────────────────────────────
   413	  //
   414	  // A reading whose citation is wrong is worse than no reading: the citation is
   415	  // what a person clicks to check it, and a wrong one survives casual review.
   416	  // The spike's worst output — a claim at 0.98 confidence attributed to a man
   417	  // who never said it — fails here and nowhere else.
   418	  //
   419	  // Only `reject`-severity problems discard. A `reclassify` one — an owner who
   420	  // did not write the message bearing the sentence — is not a defect in the
   421	  // reading, it *is* the third-party commitment case, and it routes below
   422	  // rather than dying here.
   423	  const problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[] =
   424	    messages && messages.length > 0
   425	      ? validateProposalProvenance(
   426	          {
   427	            type: proposal.type,
   428	            provenance: proposal.provenance,
   429	            quote: proposal.quote,
   430	            proposer: proposal.proposer,
   431	            attributedTo,
   432	            // The sentence being staged. Without it the quote can be verbatim,
   433	            // correctly attributed, and about something else entirely — r2's
   434	            // gauntlet, major 1.
   435	            statement: text,
   436	          },
   437	          messages,
   438	        )
   439	      : [];
   440	  const rejecting = rejectingProblems(problems);
   441	  if (rejecting.length > 0) {
   442	    return {
   443	      ...base,
   444	      verdict: 'discard',
   445	      visibility: 'none',
   446	      rule: 'provenance_failed',
   447	      reason: `demoted below θ_min: ${rejecting.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   448	    };
   449	  }
   450	
   451	  // ── Already in the room ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   452	  const duplicate = context.acceptedObjects
   453	    ? findDuplicate(proposal.type, text, proposal.provenance, context.acceptedObjects)
   454	    : null;
   455	  if (duplicate) {
   456	    return {
   457	      ...base,
   458	      verdict: 'discard',
   459	      visibility: 'none',
   460	      rule: 'duplicate_of_accepted',
   461	      duplicateOf: duplicate.objectId,
   462	      reason: `the room already accepted "${duplicate.objectId}" from the same messages, saying the same thing`,
   463	    };
   464	  }
   465	
   466	  // ── A person staged this ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
   467	  //
   468	  // θ does not apply. #4's thresholds calibrate *extraction* confidence, and a
   469	  // person's self-report is not that number — it is not a number at all. A
   470	  // human-staged reading goes to the room to be judged, which is what staging
   471	  // means. (A person writing a fact outright uses `answerBinding` or direct
   472	  // acceptance; they do not need a proposal.)
   473	  if (proposal.proposer.kind === 'human') {
   474	    // …with one exception that is not about θ at all: a person naming *somebody
   475	    // else* on a commitment is still somebody else's sentence, and #4's rule
   476	    // does not care whether a machine or a colleague wrote it. The named owner
   477	    // is the one asked, and that is what the attention panel reads to decide
   478	    // whose confirm this is.
   479	    const staged = proposal.proposer.userId;
   480	    const namesAnother =
   481	      proposal.type === 'commitment' && attributedTo !== null && attributedTo !== staged;
   482	    return {
   483	      ...base,
   484	      verdict: 'pending',
   485	      visibility: 'needs_you',
   486	      attribution: proposal.type === 'commitment' ? (namesAnother ? 'third_party' : 'self') : null,
   487	      awaitingConfirmFrom: namesAnother ? attributedTo : null,
   488	      rule: 'human_proposer',
   489	      reason: namesAnother
   490	        ? `staged by user "${staged}", who named "${attributedTo}" as owner — it waits for "${attributedTo}" to confirm; nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence (#4), whoever wrote it`
   491	        : `staged by user "${staged}" — a person's reading goes to the room to be accepted, not through θ`,
   492	    };
   493	  }
   494	
   495	  const attribution =
   496	    proposal.type === 'commitment'
   497	      ? commitmentAttribution(attributedTo ?? '', proposal.provenance, messages, proposal.quote)
   498	      : null;
   499	
   500	  // ── The receipt this check declines to rule on ───────────────────────────
   501	  //
   502	  // **Above the θ_min discard since r5, and the two cases are different
   503	  // questions rather than two heights on one scale.**
   504	  //
   505	  // r4 put this below the discard and defended it: a weak reading should be
   506	  // dropped rather than promoted into the panel by a receipt problem. That
   507	  // argument is right about *one* input and r4 applied it to two. θ_min answers
   508	  // "is this reading worth somebody's attention" — a property of the model's own
   509	  // self-report, and a weak self-report with a clean receipt is noise, so it is
   510	  // still discarded below. A `refer` answers something else entirely: **the
   511	  // receipt and the statement disagree, and the disagreement was detected.**
   512	  // That is evidence about the record, not weakness in the reading.
   513	  //
   514	  // The input that made the difference visible: quote "Bob will not deploy
   515	  // production Friday", statement "Bob will deploy production Friday",
   516	  // confidence 0.4999. r4 discarded it as too weak to show — destroying the
   517	  // exact discrepancy the receipt exists to make visible, one thousandth of a
   518	  // point below a line that has nothing to do with what was found. The
   519	  // inversion is not less real for having been proposed timidly.
   520	  //
   521	  // Still **above everything that can accept**, so there is no path from here to
   522	  // `auto_accept`.
   523	  const referring = referringProblems(problems);
   524	  if (referring.length > 0) {
   525	    return {
   526	      ...base,
   527	      attribution,
   528	      verdict: 'pending',
   529	      visibility: 'quiet',
   530	      rule: 'receipt_not_certifiable',
   531	      reason: `not accepted on a machine's word, and not thrown away: ${referring.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   532	    };
   533	  }
   534	
   535	  // ── Below the discard line ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   536	  //
   537	  // Reached only with a receipt nothing found fault with, which is what makes
   538	  // discarding safe: there is no detected discrepancy here to destroy, only a
   539	  // model that is not convinced by its own reading.
   540	  if (proposal.confidence < rule.thetaMin) {
   541	    return {
   542	      ...base,
   543	      attribution,
   544	      verdict: 'discard',
   545	      visibility: 'none',
   546	      rule: 'below_theta_min',
   547	      reason: `confidence ${proposal.confidence} is below θ_min ${rule.thetaMin} for ${proposal.type} — discarded rather than shown; an unconvincing "~" still costs someone's attention, and its receipt held, so nothing is being thrown away except a weak reading`,
   548	    };
   549	  }
   550	
   551	  // ── The band: θ_min ≤ c < θ_auto ─────────────────────────────────────────
   552	  //
   553	  // One cell for every type, decisions included. Shown quietly in current state
   554	  // as unconfirmed, never in Needs-you — #4, and the reasoning is that an
   555	  // uncertain reading is not worth a person's turn, only their glance.
   556	  if (proposal.confidence < rule.thetaAuto) {
   557	    return {
   558	      ...base,
   559	      attribution,
   560	      verdict: 'pending',
   561	      visibility: 'quiet',
   562	      rule: 'theta_band',
   563	      reason: `confidence ${proposal.confidence} is between θ_min ${rule.thetaMin} and θ_auto ${rule.thetaAuto} for ${proposal.type} — shown quietly in current state as unconfirmed, never in Needs-you unless it blocks something`,
   564	    };
   565	  }
   566	
   567	  // ── At or above θ_auto, and somebody else's name is on it ────────────────
   568	  //
   569	  // **Before the never-auto-accepts row since r5**, because r5 moved commitment
   570	  // into that row and the general refusal would otherwise have swallowed the
   571	  // specific one. Both end in `pending, needs_you`; only this one knows *whose*
   572	  // Needs-you, and `awaitingConfirmFrom` is what the attention panel reads to
   573	  // put it in front of the person being committed rather than the room.
   574	  if (proposal.type === 'commitment' && attribution === 'third_party') {
   575	    return {
   576	      ...base,
   577	      attribution,
   578	      verdict: 'pending',
   579	      visibility: 'needs_you',
   580	      awaitingConfirmFrom: attributedTo,
   581	      rule: 'third_party_commitment',
   582	      reason: `"${attributedTo}" is named as owner but did not write the message this was read out of — surfaced to them to confirm, and accepted only on that confirm; nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence (#4)`,
   583	    };
   584	  }
   585	
   586	  // ── At or above θ_auto, but this type never auto-accepts ─────────────────
   587	  //
   588	  // Three types are in this row since r5: decision (#4's absolute), commitment
   589	  // and objective (see `DEFAULT_ACCEPTANCE_RULES`). The rule is read off the
   590	  // table rather than named here, so a type joining or leaving the row changes
   591	  // one line of data.
   592	  if (!rule.autoAccept) {
   593	    return {
   594	      ...base,
   595	      attribution,
   596	      verdict: 'pending',
   597	      visibility: 'needs_you',
   598	      rule: 'never_auto_accepts',
   599	      reason: `a ${proposal.type} never auto-accepts at any confidence (#4) — at ${proposal.confidence} the pass is confident, so it goes to Needs-you for a person to accept or decline; the room's current state is unsettled until somebody rules on it`,
   600	    };
   601	  }
   602	
   603	  return {
   604	    ...base,
   605	    attribution,
   606	    verdict: 'auto_accept',
   607	    visibility: 'accepted',
   608	    rule: 'auto_accept',
   609	    reason: `confidence ${proposal.confidence} is at or above θ_auto ${rule.thetaAuto} for ${proposal.type} — accepted as "~"; nothing model-accepted renders as a fact`,
   610	  };
   611	}
   612	
   613	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   614	 * Answer-binding
   615	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
   616	
   617	/**
   618	 * "Your next message resolves it, nothing is inferred."
   619	 *
   620	 * The one path that reaches an accepted Decision without a model in it. A person
   621	 * clicks an open question, writes a reply, and that reply *is* the answer — no
   622	 * proposal is staged, no confidence is computed, nothing is extracted. #4 names
   623	 * this as one of exactly two ways a decision is ever accepted.
   624	 *
   625	 * It is a **command**, not an interpretation: this turns the command into the
     1	import type { Actor } from './common.js';
     2	import {
     3	  type ProvenanceMessage,
     4	  referringProblems,
     5	  rejectingProblems,
     6	  validateProposalProvenance,
     7	} from './escalation.js';
     8	import { hasContent, isBlank } from './matching.js';
     9	import { type AcceptedObject, type AcceptedObjectType, objectStatement } from './objects.js';
    10	import { decideSupersession, MODEL_ACCEPTANCE_FLOOR } from './policy.js';
    11	import type { Proposer, StoredProposal } from './proposal.js';
    12	import { canonicalJson } from './state.js';
    13	
    14	/**
    15	 * Who is allowed to do what, and on what evidence — the *floor* under the
    16	 * acceptance rules, enforced where nothing can route around it.
    17	 *
    18	 * Issue #4 resolved acceptance per type. Some of that matrix is policy that
    19	 * belongs to the θ engine; the rest is the trust boundary itself, and a boundary
    20	 * enforced only in the layer above the reducer is a boundary that a second
    21	 * writer, a replay, or a bug walks around. Those rows live here, and the reducer
    22	 * refuses to fold an event that breaks them.
    23	 *
    24	 * #4's matrix, and where each row is enforced:
    25	 *
    26	 * | rule (#4)                                               | who may | where     |
    27	 * | ------------------------------------------------------- | ------- | --------- |
    28	 * | Claims auto-accept at confidence ≥ θ                     | model   | **here**  |
    29	 * | OpenQuestions auto-accept at confidence ≥ θ              | model   | **here**  |
    30	 * | **Commitments never auto-accept** (r5)                   | human   | **here**  |
    31	 * | **Objectives never auto-accept** (r5)                    | human   | **here**  |
    32	 * | **Decisions never auto-accept**                          | human   | **here**  |
    33	 * | A claim becomes `verified`                               | human   | **here**  |
    34	 * | Supersession, split by the type being retired            | per policy | **here** |
    35	 * | **Corrections (amend / retract / restore / …)**          | human   | **here**  |
    36	 * | **Acceptance citing no proposal at all**                 | human   | **here**  |
    37	 * | **Declaring a question answered**                        | human   | **here**  |
    38	 *
    39	 * ## What round 2 moved down here, and why
    40	 *
    41	 * Every row above that used to say "#21 (θ)" or "the engine" now says **here**.
    42	 * Round 1's gauntlet found the same shape twice:
    43	 *
    44	 *  1. **The actor was forgeable.** Every gate below reads an actor, and the
    45	 *     actor used to be part of the event payload — so a worker could declare
    46	 *     itself human and walk through all of them. The actor now arrives out of
    47	 *     band (`TrustedContext`), derived from the authenticated session, and the
    48	 *     event schema has nowhere to put one.
    49	 *  2. **The receipt checks were opt-in.** θ, quote-matching, and "did the person
    50	 *     named actually say this" lived in `acceptance.ts`, which the layer that
    51	 *     mints events calls *if it remembers to* and which returned "no problems"
    52	 *     when handed no messages. A check that fails open when its input is missing
    53	 *     is a manner, not a boundary. They are conditions of folding now:
    54	 *     `acceptanceReceiptRefusal` runs on every non-human acceptance, and an
    55	 *     acceptance whose messages the reducer cannot see is refused rather than
    56	 *     waved through.
    57	 *
    58	 * Two things are still deliberately left open, so the omission is a decision:
    59	 *  - `proposal_recorded` is open to every actor. Recording a reading is not
    60	 *    asserting it; the whole trust model is that staging is free and acceptance
    61	 *    is not.
    62	 *  - `proposal_rejected` is not a correction verb — withdrawing a staged reading
    63	 *    destroys nothing — but it is narrowed by `ProposalBindingGate`: a model may
    64	 *    retire *its own* reading, not somebody else's.
    65	 *
    66	 * `acceptance.ts` still exists and is still stricter: it runs over richer inputs
    67	 * (what the room already accepted, the whole window) and decides what a worker
    68	 * should emit at all. It reads the same θ table this file does, so the two
    69	 * cannot invert.
    70	 *
    71	 * ## What this floor enforces *independently*, and what it shares
    72	 *
    73	 * r4's receipt claimed two boundaries without qualification, and r4's blind
    74	 * review was right to refuse it: `acceptanceReceiptRefusal` below calls the same
    75	 * `validateProposalProvenance` the engine does, so a defect in quote semantics
    76	 * is a defect in both. The claim is scoped rather than repeated:
    77	 *
    78	 *  - **Independent of the engine, and unreachable from it**: the actor gates
    79	 *    above; that a cited proposal exists, is open, and matches the object's type
    80	 *    and room; that a non-human acted on its own reading; `MODEL_ACCEPTANCE_FLOOR`;
    81	 *    payload equality; provenance equality; that a window exists; that a quote
    82	 *    exists. Delete `acceptance.ts` entirely and every one of those still holds.
    83	 *  - **Shared, by design**: what the quote *means* — one implementation, two
    84	 *    call sites, because two implementations of "does this quote bear this
    85	 *    sentence" would be two answers to one question and `policy.ts` exists to
    86	 *    say why that is the defect rather than the redundancy.
    87	 */
    88	export type HumanOnlyGate =
    89	  /** `object_accepted` with `provenance.proposalId === null`. */
    90	  | 'direct_acceptance'
    91	  /** `object_accepted` for a `decision`, proposal or no proposal. */
    92	  | 'decision_acceptance'
    93	  /**
    94	   * `object_accepted` for a `commitment`. **r5.** #44's fact-check drove a model
    95	   * actor through the whole stack with `{statement: 'Bob will deploy production
    96	   * Friday', owner: 'user_bob'}` and it landed with zero issues: the quote was
    97	   * real, the author was real, and the attribution rules read it as self-stated
    98	   * because the bearing message's author id equalled the owner. Nothing asked
    99	   * whether a machine may put an obligation on a named person at all.
   100	   */
   101	  | 'commitment_acceptance'
   102	  /**
   103	   * `object_accepted` for an `objective`. **r5.** An objective is what
   104	   * everything else is filed under, and `decideSupersession` already reserves
   105	   * *retiring* one to a person — a gate on the way out and none on the way in is
   106	   * a front door with the back door open.
   107	   */
   108	  | 'objective_acceptance'
   109	  /** Any transition of a claim to `verification: 'verified'`. */
   110	  | 'claim_verification'
   111	  /** `supersedes` pointed at a type the supersession policy reserves to people. */
   112	  | 'supersession'
   113	  /** An `answers` edge — declaring a question settled. */
   114	  | 'answer_relation'
   115	  /** `object_corrected`, every verb. */
   116	  | 'correction';
   117	
   118	/**
   119	 * The gate a non-human actor hits when it tries to mint this type, or `null`
   120	 * when the type is one a machine may mint.
   121	 *
   122	 * **Derived from the same θ table `MODEL_ACCEPTANCE_FLOOR` is derived from**, so
   123	 * the named refusal and the unreachable number cannot drift: a type that stops
   124	 * auto-accepting acquires a gate here in the same commit, and `policy.test.ts`
   125	 * asserts the two agree for every type rather than trusting this switch.
   126	 *
   127	 * A gate *and* a floor for the same rule is deliberate. The floor alone would
   128	 * refuse these acceptances — `+Infinity` is unreachable — but it would refuse
   129	 * them as "confidence 0.95 is below the floor of Infinity", which tells a room
   130	 * nothing about why. `RETRO.md`: a rule applied at one site is not a rule, and a
   131	 * refusal that does not name itself is a dead end.
   132	 */
   133	export function modelMintingGate(type: AcceptedObjectType): HumanOnlyGate | null {
   134	  switch (type) {
   135	    case 'decision':
   136	      return 'decision_acceptance';
   137	    case 'commitment':
   138	      return 'commitment_acceptance';
   139	    case 'objective':
   140	      return 'objective_acceptance';
   141	    case 'claim':
   142	    case 'open_question':
   143	      return null;
   144	    default: {
   145	      const exhaustive: never = type;
   146	      // A type nobody has classified is not a type a machine may mint.
   147	      return JSON.stringify(exhaustive) === '' ? null : 'direct_acceptance';
   148	    }
   149	  }
   150	}

import {
  AcceptedObject,
  type AcceptedObjectInput,
  type Actor,
  type AppendResult,
  type AuthoredEvent,
  appendEvent,
  authored,
  type CoreEvent,
  type CoreEventInput,
  CoreEvent as CoreEventSchema,
  type CoreState,
  emptyProvenance,
  type ProvenanceMessage,
} from '../src/index.js';

export const ROOM = 'room_1';
export const ALICE = 'user_alice';
export const BOB = 'user_bob';

/** Fixed timestamps — the core never reads a clock, so tests never need one. */
export function at(minute: number): string {
  const mm = String(minute).padStart(2, '0');
  return `2026-07-31T10:${mm}:00.000Z`;
}

export function object(input: AcceptedObjectInput) {
  return AcceptedObject.parse(input);
}

/** The raw payload, with no actor anywhere near it. */
export function payload(input: CoreEventInput): CoreEvent {
  return CoreEventSchema.parse(input);
}

/**
 * One ledger row, the way a command layer produces it: the payload, plus the
 * actor it authenticated and the messages it can show for the receipt.
 *
 * The `actor` key here is **not** part of the event — this helper is standing in
 * for the session lookup #22 will do, and it splits the actor out into the
 * trusted slot before the payload is ever parsed. Putting one in the payload is
 * a parse error, which `guards.test.ts` pins.
 */
export function event(
  input: CoreEventInput & { actor: Actor; messages?: readonly ProvenanceMessage[] },
): AuthoredEvent {
  const { actor, messages, ...rest } = input as Record<string, unknown> & {
    actor: Actor;
    messages?: readonly ProvenanceMessage[];
  };
  return authored(CoreEventSchema.parse(rest), {
    actor,
    ...(messages === undefined ? {} : { messages }),
  });
}

/**
 * A ledger row whose payload is **not parsed** — the shape a command layer
 * actually hands over when the event came off a wire, out of a jsonb column, or
 * through any call site TypeScript could not see.
 *
 * This is the one deliberate cast in the test suite, and it exists because r2's
 * gauntlet found that its absence was the whole gap: `event()` above parses
 * every fixture, so the tests proved the *schemas* refuse things while proving
 * nothing about whether the reducer ever runs them. Rows built here go in raw,
 * exactly as an untyped caller's would.
 */
export function rawEvent(
  payload: unknown,
  trusted: { actor: Actor; messages?: readonly ProvenanceMessage[] },
): AuthoredEvent {
  return authored(payload as CoreEvent, trusted);
}

/** `appendEvent` for a ledger row — the payload and its trusted context, split. */
export function append(state: CoreState, entry: AuthoredEvent): AppendResult {
  return appendEvent(state, entry.event, entry);
}

/** The event ids of a log, in order. */
export function ids(log: readonly AuthoredEvent[]): string[] {
  return log.map((entry) => entry.event.id);
}

/** The same row, re-minted at a different position — a redelivery, as the wire makes one. */
export function reminted(
  entry: AuthoredEvent,
  changes: { at?: string; id?: string },
): AuthoredEvent {
  return {
    ...entry,
    event: {
      ...entry.event,
      ...(changes.at === undefined ? {} : { at: changes.at }),
      ...(changes.id === undefined ? {} : { id: changes.id }),
    },
  };
}

export const human = (userId = ALICE) => ({ kind: 'human', userId }) as const;
export const model = (name = 'test-model') => ({ kind: 'model', model: name }) as const;
export const system = () => ({ kind: 'system' }) as const;

/**
 * The room's messages, as the trusted context supplies them.
 *
 * `msg_1` is ALICE's own words and `msg_3` is BOB's, so a claim of ALICE's
 * quoting `msg_1` has a receipt that holds and a commitment owned by BOB quoting
 * `msg_3` is self-stated. `msg_2` is BOB quoting ALICE — the blockquote shape
 * that makes a receipt look right and be wrong.
 */
export const MESSAGES: ProvenanceMessage[] = [
  { id: 'msg_1', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Ship the scaffold behind a flag, I think.' },
  { id: 'msg_2', authorId: BOB, body: '> Ship the scaffold behind a flag, I think.\n\nAgreed.' },
  { id: 'msg_3', authorId: BOB, body: "I'll wire the flag into the server tomorrow." },
  { id: 'msg_4', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Do we keep the flag after launch?' },
  { id: 'msg_5', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Drop the flag; ship it on.' },
];

/**
 * A small but complete log: a model proposes a decision, a human accepts it,
 * amends the wording, opens a commitment and a question, then a second decision
 * supersedes the first and answers the question.
 */
export function sampleLog(): AuthoredEvent[] {

        provenance: { messageIds: ['msg_1', 'msg_2'], proposalId: 'prop_1' },
        createdAt: at(2),
        updatedAt: at(2),
      },
    }),
    event({
import { z } from 'zod';
import { Id, Timestamp } from './common.js';
import { isBlank } from './matching.js';

import {
  ClaimPayload,
  CommitmentPayload,
  DecisionPayload,
  ObjectivePayload,
  OpenQuestionPayload,
} from './objects.js';

/**
 * Pre-acceptance staging. A proposal is what the interpreter *thinks* happened.
 * It renders as `~`, never as a fact, until a human accepts it — that boundary
 * is the whole trust model (init.md §5).
 */

export const Proposer = z.discriminatedUnion('kind', [
  z.object({ kind: z.literal('model'), model: z.string().min(1) }),
  z.object({ kind: z.literal('human'), userId: Id }),
]);
export type Proposer = z.infer<typeof Proposer>;

export const ProposalStatus = z.enum(['proposed', 'accepted', 'rejected', 'superseded']);
export type ProposalStatus = z.infer<typeof ProposalStatus>;

const proposalBase = z.object({
  id: Id,
  roomId: Id,
  /** Model self-reported confidence, 0..1. Acceptance rules read this. */
  confidence: z.number().min(0).max(1),
  proposer: Proposer,
  /**
   * Source messages this reading was drawn from. **Enforced non-empty for model
   * proposers** by the refinement below — see `Proposal`.
   */
  provenance: z.array(Id).default([]),
  /**
   * The span of a cited message this reading rests on, verbatim. Optional
   * because a human staging a proposal is not quoting anything, but when a model
   * supplies one it is checkable, and `validateProposalProvenance` checks it:
   * the quote must appear in a cited message *after* GitHub reply-blockquotes
   * are stripped. The interpretation spike's worst error — a claim attributed to
   * the wrong person at 0.98 confidence — was a quote that only existed inside
   * somebody else's reply-blockquote, and nothing but this field makes that
   * detectable without a model.
   */
  quote: z.string().nullable().default(null),
  interpretationId: Id.nullable().default(null),
  status: ProposalStatus.default('proposed'),
  createdAt: Timestamp,
});

/** The payload is validated against the same schema the accepted object will use. */
const ProposalShape = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
  proposalBase.extend({ type: z.literal('decision'), payload: DecisionPayload }),
  proposalBase.extend({ type: z.literal('commitment'), payload: CommitmentPayload }),
  proposalBase.extend({ type: z.literal('open_question'), payload: OpenQuestionPayload }),
  proposalBase.extend({ type: z.literal('claim'), payload: ClaimPayload }),
  proposalBase.extend({ type: z.literal('objective'), payload: ObjectivePayload }),
]);

/**
 * The proposal, with the two rules that could not be expressed field-by-field.
 *
 * **1. A model proposal must cite at least one message.** This was a comment on
 * `provenance` and nothing else — routed out of #19's gauntlet, because a
 * comment is not a constraint. Provenance is the receipt: it is what the UI
 * shows under a `~`, what `validateProposalProvenance` checks a quote against,
 * and what a person clicks to decide whether the reading is right. A model
 * proposal with no receipt is an assertion, which is the one thing the
 * acceptance boundary exists to refuse. A *human* proposer may cite nothing —
 * a person staging their own reading is the receipt.
 *
 * **2. A model proposal must quote the message it was read out of — every type.**
 * r1's gauntlet found the attribution check was satisfied by the owner having
 * authored *any* cited message, so padding `provenance` with one message the
 * owner happened to write flipped a third-party commitment into a self-statement.
 * The fix binds attribution to the message that carries the sentence, and the
 * only thing that identifies that message is the quote.
 *
 * That argument scoped the rule to claims and commitments, the two types that put
 * a name on somebody, and **r3's gauntlet found the scope was the hole**: a model
 * objective with `quote: null` cited one message with an empty body and reached
 * accepted state with no receipt check running at all. Attribution is one thing
 * the quote answers; the other is "which sentence, in which message", and every
 * type is minted from a sentence. So the quote is mandatory for all five —
 * without it there is no answer to what this reading rests on, and the honest
 * reading of no answer is that it rests on nothing.
 *
 * Emptiness is `isBlank`: a quote of `"​"` or `"…"` carries no words and is
 * the same absence as `null`, checked in the one place that decides what content
 * is rather than by a `trim()` that only knows about spaces.
 */
export const Proposal = ProposalShape.superRefine((proposal, ctx) => {
  if (proposal.proposer.kind !== 'model') return;
  if (proposal.provenance.length === 0) {
    ctx.addIssue({
      code: 'custom',
      path: ['provenance'],
      message:
        'a model proposal must cite at least one source message — a reading with no receipt cannot be checked, and an unbacked `~` is an assertion',
    });
  }
  if (isBlank(proposal.quote)) {
    ctx.addIssue({
      code: 'custom',
      path: ['quote'],
      message: `a model ${proposal.type} proposal must quote the message that carries it — a receipt names the sentence it rests on, and without the quote there is nothing to identify it`,
    });
  }
});

export type Proposal = z.infer<typeof Proposal>;
export type ProposalInput = z.input<typeof Proposal>;

/** The union without the cross-field rule, for callers that need `.options`. */
export const ProposalVariants = ProposalShape;

/** `Omit` that distributes over a union, so the `type` discriminator survives. */
type DistributiveOmit<T, K extends PropertyKey> = T extends unknown ? Omit<T, K> : never;

/**
 * A proposal as the reducer stores it: everything the event carried *except*
 * `status`.
 *
 * Status is lifecycle, and lifecycle belongs to the log — `proposal_rejected`
 * and `object_accepted` move it. Keeping a second, mutable copy inside the
 * stored proposal gave two answers to one question, and only one of them was
 * ever updated. `ProposalRecord.status` is the only status there is; use
 * `proposalWithStatus` when a caller wants the whole proposal back.
 */
export type StoredProposal = DistributiveOmit<Proposal, 'status'>;

/** Drops the wire status so the record cannot hold a stale second copy of it. */
export function storeProposal(proposal: Proposal): StoredProposal {
  const { status: _status, ...rest } = proposal;
  return rest as StoredProposal;
}

   360	export function decideAcceptance(
   361	  proposal: Proposal | StoredProposal,
   362	  context: AcceptanceContext,
   363	): AcceptanceDecision {
   364	  const config = context.config ?? defaultAcceptanceConfig;
   365	  const rule = config[proposal.type];
   366	  const payload = proposal.payload as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
   367	  const text = payloadText(proposal.type, payload);
   368	  const attributedTo = payloadAttributedTo(proposal.type, payload);
   369	  const messages = context.messages as readonly ProvenanceMessage[] | undefined;
   370	
   371	  const base = {
   372	    thetaAuto: rule.thetaAuto,
   373	    thetaMin: rule.thetaMin,
   374	    autoAcceptAvailable: rule.autoAccept,
   375	    attribution: null,
   376	    duplicateOf: null,
   377	    awaitingConfirmFrom: null,
   378	  } as const;
   379	
   380	  // ── No window, no verdict ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   381	  //
   382	  // Fail-closed, and loudly: this is a caller bug, not a reading defect. The
   383	  // failure it replaces was silent in the other direction.
   384	  //
   385	  // **Empty counts as absent.** Round 2's gauntlet found the `undefined`-only
   386	  // form of this check: `messages: []` walked past the door marked "required",
   387	  // and everything downstream then found nothing wrong because there was nothing
   388	  // to look in. The two spellings describe the same state of the world — nobody
   389	  // supplied the messages this reading cites — so they get the same answer.
   390	  //
   391	  // **And empty of content counts as empty.** Round 3's gauntlet found the
   392	  // `length`-only form: one message whose `body` is `""` makes the array
   393	  // non-empty and supplies nothing, which is the same trick one level in. The
   394	  // question is whether anybody supplied words, so the test is `hasContent`.
   395	  const windowIsAbsent =
   396	    messages === undefined || messages.length === 0 || !messages.some((m) => hasContent(m.body));
   397	  if (proposal.proposer.kind === 'model' && windowIsAbsent) {
   398	    return {
   399	      ...base,
   400	      verdict: 'discard',
   401	      visibility: 'none',
   402	      rule: 'missing_message_context',
   403	      reason:
   404	        messages === undefined
   405	          ? 'no message window was supplied, so the receipt could not be checked — a model reading is never accepted on trust; supply the messages it cites'
   406	          : messages.length === 0
   407	            ? 'an empty message window was supplied, so the receipt could not be checked — an empty window is not a window; a model reading is never accepted on trust'
   408	            : `a message window of ${messages.length} message${messages.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} was supplied and not one of them has any words in it, so the receipt could not be checked — a window with nothing in it is not a window; a model reading is never accepted on trust`,
   409	    };
   410	  }
   411	
   412	  // ── The receipt, before anything else ────────────────────────────────────
   413	  //
   414	  // A reading whose citation is wrong is worse than no reading: the citation is
   415	  // what a person clicks to check it, and a wrong one survives casual review.
   416	  // The spike's worst output — a claim at 0.98 confidence attributed to a man
   417	  // who never said it — fails here and nowhere else.
   418	  //
   419	  // Only `reject`-severity problems discard. A `reclassify` one — an owner who
   420	  // did not write the message bearing the sentence — is not a defect in the
   421	  // reading, it *is* the third-party commitment case, and it routes below
   422	  // rather than dying here.
   423	  const problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[] =
   424	    messages && messages.length > 0
   425	      ? validateProposalProvenance(
   426	          {
   427	            type: proposal.type,
   428	            provenance: proposal.provenance,
   429	            quote: proposal.quote,
   430	            proposer: proposal.proposer,
   431	            attributedTo,
   432	            // The sentence being staged. Without it the quote can be verbatim,
   433	            // correctly attributed, and about something else entirely — r2's
   434	            // gauntlet, major 1.
   435	            statement: text,
   436	          },
   437	          messages,
   438	        )
   439	      : [];
   440	  const rejecting = rejectingProblems(problems);
   441	  if (rejecting.length > 0) {
   442	    return {
   443	      ...base,
   444	      verdict: 'discard',
   445	      visibility: 'none',
   446	      rule: 'provenance_failed',
   447	      reason: `demoted below θ_min: ${rejecting.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   448	    };
   449	  }
   450	
   451	  // ── Already in the room ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   452	  const duplicate = context.acceptedObjects
   453	    ? findDuplicate(proposal.type, text, proposal.provenance, context.acceptedObjects)
   454	    : null;
   455	  if (duplicate) {
   456	    return {
   457	      ...base,
   458	      verdict: 'discard',
   459	      visibility: 'none',
   460	      rule: 'duplicate_of_accepted',
   461	      duplicateOf: duplicate.objectId,
   462	      reason: `the room already accepted "${duplicate.objectId}" from the same messages, saying the same thing`,
   463	    };
   464	  }
   465

  1116	
  1117	/**
  1118	 * **Does a message later in this window say the quoted sentence again, with
  1119	 * something added?**
  1120	 *
  1121	 * "Later" is positional: after every message the proposal cites. `EscalationWindow`
  1122	 * documents its messages as being in room order, and the acceptance path is
  1123	 * handed the same window, so position is the ordering the room itself has.
  1124	 * Messages the proposal cites are not later than themselves, and a message
  1125	 * *before* the citations is not a revision of them.
  1126	 *
  1127	 * The comparison runs over **routing** tokens rather than receipt tokens, and
  1128	 * that is the one place on the receipt path where the lossy fold is correct: this
  1129	 * is a detector, not a certification. A false positive costs a referral — a
  1130	 * person reads two sentences — and a false negative costs an auto-accepted
  1131	 * statement the room has already walked back. Case, fullwidth spellings and
  1132	 * markdown are all noise to that question.
  1133	 *
  1134	 * Returns the tokens the later message added, because a refusal that does not
  1135	 * say what changed is a dead end.
  1136	 */
  1137	export function laterRevision(
  1138	  statement: string,
  1139	  citedIds: readonly string[],
  1140	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1141	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
  1142	): { message: ProvenanceMessage; added: string[] } | 'unscanned' | null {
  1143	  if (isBlank(statement)) return null;
  1144	  const wanted = routingTokens(statement);
  1145	  if (wanted.length === 0) return null;
  1146	  // Too long to align against anything is the same answer the bearing check
  1147	  // gives: not checked, therefore not passed.
  1148	  if (wanted.length > policy.maxAlignedTokens) return 'unscanned';
  1149	
  1150	  const cited = new Set(citedIds);
  1151	  let lastCited = -1;
  1152	  for (const [index, message] of messages.entries()) {
  1153	    if (cited.has(message.id)) lastCited = index;
  1154	  }
  1155	  if (lastCited === -1) return null;
  1156	
  1157	  const later = messages.slice(lastCited + 1);
  1158	  // ── The cap fails closed, and that is r5 auditing its own first draft ──────
  1159	  //
  1160	  // The first version of this scan stopped at the cap and returned "nothing
  1161	  // found", with a comment arguing that stopping was safe "in the direction that
  1162	  // matters — it can only miss a correction". That argument is the exact shape
  1163	  // this round exists to delete: a limit written into a comment, and an input
  1164	  // inside it **auto-accepted**. A window with 201 messages after the citation
  1165	  // is a window this function did not read, and an unread window is not a clean
  1166	  // one. `maxAlignedTokens` and `maxScannedSentences` already answer their own
  1167	  // version of this question with a refusal; this one now does too.
  1168	  if (later.length > policy.maxLaterMessagesScanned) return 'unscanned';
  1169	
  1170	  for (const message of later) {
  1171	    const own = stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body);
  1172	    const sentences = sentencesOf(own);
  1173	    if (sentences.length > policy.maxScannedSentences) return 'unscanned';
  1174	    for (const sentence of sentences) {
  1175	      const aligned = alignTokens(routingTokens(sentence), wanted, policy);
  1176	      if (aligned.undecidable === 'too_long') return 'unscanned';
  1177	      if (aligned.undecidable !== null) continue;
  1178	      // Every word of the statement is here, in order, and this sentence says
  1179	      // more. An exact restatement (`unmatchedInQuote` empty) is agreement, and
  1180	      // a sentence missing any of the statement's words is about something else.
  1181	      if (aligned.unmatchedInStatement.length === 0 && aligned.unmatchedInQuote.length > 0) {
  1182	        return { message, added: aligned.unmatchedInQuote };
  1183	      }
  1184	    }
  1185	  }
  1186	  return null;
  1187	}
  1188	
  1189	/**
  1190	 * Every cited message whose *own* text carries a quoted span — reply-blockquotes
  1191	 * stripped, so a message that merely quoted the sentence back does not count.
  1192	 *
  1193	 * Returns all of them rather than the first, because "how many" is the question
  1194	 * `ambiguous_quote` asks and a function that returns one answer cannot be asked
  1195	 * it.
  1196	 */
  1197	export function bearingMessages(
  1198	  quote: string,
  1199	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
  1200	): ProvenanceMessage[] {
  1201	  const normalized = normalizeForReceipt(quote);
  1202	  if (normalized.length === 0) return [];
  1203	  return messages.filter((message) =>
  1204	    normalizeForReceipt(stripReplyBlockquotes(message.body)).includes(normalized),
  1205	  );
   285	   * meaning — `?` and `!` are *not* here, because "Bob will deploy Friday?" is a
   286	   * question and minting it as an assertion is the same defect in different
   287	   * clothes. Adding anything to this set changes what the product guarantees; the
   288	   * bar is an argument nobody can break, and three of the four entries that used
   289	   * to be here did not clear it.
   290	   */
   291	  droppableTokens: ReadonlySet<string>;
   292	  /**
   293	   * The most tokens the bearing alignment will compare on either side.
   294	   *
   295	   * The alignment resynchronises with a lookahead, so it is O(n·m) in the worst
   296	   * case and `quote` arrives from the same untrusted place every other field
   297	   * does. Above this the check does not degrade, it **refuses** — an input too
   298	   * large to align is an input that has not been checked, and the honest answer
   299	   * to that is the same as every other unanswerable question here.
   300	   */
   301	  maxAlignedTokens: number;
   302	  /**
   303	   * Sentences of one message the quote-anchoring check will scan.
   304	   *
   305	   * Same argument as `maxAlignedTokens`: the check is quadratic in the sentence
   306	   * count and the message body is somebody else's input. Above this it refuses.
   307	   */
   308	  maxScannedSentences: number;
   309	  /**
   310	   * Messages *after* the cited ones that the later-correction scan will read.
   311	   *
   312	   * r5: `ProvenanceMessage` carried `id`, `authorId` and `body`, and validation
   313	   * constructed and inspected only the **cited** messages — so a window holding
   314	   * `"We will deploy production Friday."` followed by `"Correction: we will not
   315	   * deploy production Friday."`, with the proposal citing only the first,
   316	   * auto-accepted. The message schema being append-only prevents a literal edit;
   317	   * it does not make a later correction part of acceptance.
   318	   *
   319	   * Bounded for the same reason every other scan here is: the window is somebody
   320	   * else's input and the scan is linear in it times the sentences in each. Above
   321	   * this the scan stops, and stopping is safe in the direction that matters —
   322	   * it can only miss a correction, and a missed correction leaves the receipt
   323	   * exactly as strong as it was in r4.
   324	   */
   325	  maxLaterMessagesScanned: number;
   326	}
   327	
   328	export const RECEIPT_POLICY: Readonly<ReceiptPolicy> = Object.freeze({
   329	  minQuoteLength: 24,
   330	  droppableTokens: Object.freeze(new Set(['.'])) as ReadonlySet<string>,
   331	  maxAlignedTokens: 400,
   332	  maxScannedSentences: 200,
   333	  maxLaterMessagesScanned: 200,
   334	});

   337	 * Supersession
   338	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

   340	export type SupersessionAuthority =
   341	  /** A model may land it. */
   342	  | 'auto_accept'
   343	  /** A person must. */
   344	  | 'requires_human';
