[32m succeeded in 0ms:[0m
   260	 * matcher: two different decisions drawn from one message share provenance
   261	 * completely, and two readings of one subsystem share most of their words.
   262	 *
   263	 * ## Why this is not a similarity score any more
   264	 *
   265	 * **r5, from the audit of what every documented limit does with an input inside
   266	 * it — and this one was not on anybody's list.** `escalation.ts` says of its
   267	 * stopword table, in bold: *"This list decides which model reads a window. It
   268	 * has not decided whether a reading becomes a fact since r4, and it must never
   269	 * do so again."* It still did, through here. This function measured similarity
   270	 * with `contentTokens`, which drops stopwords, deduplicates and imposes a
   271	 * three-character floor — and `not`, `all` and `some` are all on that list. A
   272	 * duplicate is **discarded**, so:
   273	 *
   274	 * | already accepted, from msg_1     | newly read, from msg_1              | r4 verdict |
   275	 * | -------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ---------- |
   276	 * | The migration is reversible      | The migration is **not** reversible | discarded  |
   277	 * | **All** services restart cleanly | **Some** services restart cleanly   | discarded  |
   278	 *
   279	 * That is r3's gauntlet finding — a similarity measure accepting the negation of
   280	 * the thing it validates — surviving in the one path nobody re-read, and it is
   281	 * *worse* here than there: the reading is not referred or refused, it is
   282	 * destroyed, and what it was contradicting stays on the record. A correction the
   283	 * room can never see is the failure this whole ticket exists to prevent.
   284	 *
   285	 * So the test is the alignment the receipt already uses, over routing tokens: a
   286	 * duplicate is a **re-proposal of the same sentence**, with nothing added and
   287	 * nothing dropped on either side. `duplicateThreshold` is gone rather than
   288	 * tuned — `RETRO.md`: when a value invites a bypass, deleting it invites
   289	 * neither. A near-duplicate now costs a person one click, which is the cheap
   290	 * side of this trade; the expensive side deleted a contradiction in silence.
   291	 *
   292	 * Routing tokens rather than receipt tokens, deliberately: case, fullwidth
   293	 * spellings and markdown are noise to "is this the same sentence again", and
   294	 * being lossy *here* means firing more often, which for the receipt path is the
   295	 * dangerous direction and for a re-proposal is the harmless one — a text that
   296	 * differs only in case is the same reading twice.
   297	 */
   298	export function findDuplicate(
   299	  type: AcceptedObjectType,
   300	  text: string,
   301	  messageIds: readonly Id[],
   302	  accepted: readonly AcceptedObjectRef[],
   303	): AcceptedObjectRef | null {
   304	  const wanted = routingTokens(text);
   305	  if (wanted.length === 0) return null;
   306	  const cited = new Set(messageIds);
   307	  for (const candidate of accepted) {
   308	    if (candidate.type !== type) continue;
   309	    if (!candidate.messageIds.some((id) => cited.has(id))) continue;
   310	    const have = routingTokens(candidate.text);
   311	    if (have.length === 0) continue;
   312	    // Symmetric by construction: `borne` requires both sides accounted for, so
   313	    // neither "the new one restates the old" nor the reverse is enough alone.
   314	    if (alignTokens(have, wanted).borne) return candidate;
   315	  }
   316	  return null;
   317	}

   319	/**
   320	 * #4's matrix, applied to one staged reading.
   321	 *
   322	 * The table this implements, exactly — one row per cell, and
   323	 * `acceptance.test.ts` has one test per row:
   324	 *
   325	 * | type                     | c < θ_min | θ_min ≤ c < θ_auto | c ≥ θ_auto            |
   326	 * | ------------------------ | --------- | ------------------ | --------------------- |
   327	 * | claim                    | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   328	 * | open_question            | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   329	 * | objective                | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   330	 * | commitment, self-stated  | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept           |
   331	 * | commitment, third-party  | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, owner confirm|
   332	 * | decision                 | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, Needs-you    |
   333	 *
   334	 * …with one cell in front of all of them: **no window, no verdict**. A model
   335	 * proposal judged without the messages it cites is discarded, because the
   336	 * alternative — an empty problem set read as a clean receipt — is how a wrong
   337	 * citation becomes an accepted fact.
   338	 *
   339	 * …and one cell across all of them, above θ_min: **a receipt nothing can rule
   340	 * on is pending, quiet**. Not accepted, because a machine may not act on a
   341	 * quote it cannot certify; not discarded, because the discrepancy is exactly
   342	 * what a person needs to see. `receipt_not_certifiable`.
   343	 *
   344	 * Two cells are worth defending.
   345	 *

   755	/**
   756	 * The three deterministic post-checks the spike specified, plus the two
   757	 * structural ones they imply. Every one of these caught a real error in the
   758	 * spike, and none of them needs a model.
   759	 *
   760	 * A proposal with any problem should be **demoted below θ_min** — discarded,
   761	 * not surfaced (`acceptance.ts` does this when the problems are passed in). The
   762	 * reasoning is the whole point of the receipt: a reading whose citation is
   763	 * wrong is worse than no reading, because the citation is what a person clicks
   764	 * to check it, and a wrong one survives casual review.
   765	 */
   766	export function validateProposalProvenance(
   767	  subject: ProvenanceSubject,
   768	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[],
   769	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   770	): ProvenanceProblem[] {
   771	  const problems: ProvenanceProblem[] = [];
   772	  const byId = new Map(messages.map((message) => [message.id, message]));
   773	
   774	  // The strictness below is aimed at *machine* readings. A person staging their
   775	  // own reading is the receipt (#4) and is not asked to quote themselves, so the
   776	  // checks that would demand a quote of them are scoped rather than universal.
   777	  const fromModel = subject.proposer?.kind === 'model';
   778	
   779	  if (subject.provenance.length === 0) {
   780	    if (fromModel) {
   781	      problems.push({
   782	        kind: 'no_provenance',
   783	        severity: 'reject',
   784	        detail: 'a model proposal must cite at least one source message',
   785	        messageId: null,
packages/core/src/escalation.ts:976:      const revisited = laterRevision(
packages/core/src/escalation.ts:1144:export function laterRevision(

   471	
   472	describe('validateProposalProvenance — the spike’s three post-checks', () => {
   473	  const messages = [messageOpener, messageQuotingOpener, messageDispute].map((message) => ({
   474	    id: message.id,
   475	    authorId: message.authorId,
   476	    body: message.body,
   477	  }));
   478	
   479	  /**
   480	   * The whole final sentence of `messageDispute`.
   481	   *
   482	   * r4: a compliant quote is one or more **whole sentences** of the cited
   483	   * message. These fixtures used to quote a span out of the middle of this
   484	   * sentence, which is the shape r4's own blind review turned into an
   485	   * auto-accept of an inverted claim ("it is not true that …" left outside the
   486	   * scissors), so they quote the sentence.
   487	   */
   488	  const DISPUTE_SENTENCE =
   489	    "While TypeScript is correct that `this.state` must be `'online'` immediately after line 5, " +
   490	    'line 6 (`this.bar()`) changes that, so TypeScript *should not* be so confident that ' +
   491	    "`this.state` is still `'online'` after line 6.";
   492	  /**
   493	   * The same sentence with the emphasis a model drops while quoting, **written
   494	   * out** rather than computed.
   495	   *
   496	   * r4's blind review, third occurrence of the derived-probe class in this
   497	   * ticket (after r3's θ probes and r4's `maxAlignedTokens + 1`): this constant
   498	   * used to read `normalizeForReceipt(DISPUTE_SENTENCE)` and was then used as the
   499	   * *expected* value below. Delete the emphasis rule from the normalizer and the
   500	   * fixture and the assertion move together — the test goes on passing while the
   501	   * behaviour it names is gone. A probe computed by the code under test cannot
   502	   * fail on that code.
   503	   *
   504	   * The backticks survive on purpose: since r5 a code span is content, not
   505	   * formatting, so a model that drops *those* has changed the sentence. What it
   506	   * may drop is the paired `*…*`, and that is the only difference between this
   507	   * string and the one above.
   508	   */
   509	  const DISPUTE_SENTENCE_PLAIN =
   510	    "While TypeScript is correct that `this.state` must be `'online'` immediately after line 5, " +
   511	    'line 6 (`this.bar()`) changes that, so TypeScript should not be so confident that ' +
   512	    "`this.state` is still `'online'` after line 6.";
   513	
   514	  const problemKinds = (...args: Parameters<typeof validateProposalProvenance>) =>
   515	    validateProposalProvenance(...args)
   516	      .map((problem) => problem.kind)
   517	      .sort();
   518	
   519	  it('catches the worst error in the whole spike', () => {
   520	    // A Claim attributed to jordanbtucker, citing dhlolo's message, quoting a
   521	    // sentence that is in it only because dhlolo blockquoted him. Claim text
   522	    // right, claimant right, provenance overlap non-empty, receipt wrong — and
   523	    // a naive substring matcher scores it as a pass.
   524	    const problems = validateProposalProvenance(
   525	      {

   300	
   301	      expect(decision.verdict).toBe(cell.verdict);

   345	     * rule name the type declares" passed while one rule was unreachable from
   346	     * it, and would have gone on passing if that engine behaviour were deleted.
   347	     * A hand-written list is blind to a name that appears; deriving it from the

     1	import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
     2	import {
     3	  AcceptedObject as AcceptedObjectSchema,
     4	  type AcceptedObjectType,
     5	  type Actor,
     6	  type AuthoredEvent,
     7	  acceptanceReceiptRefusal,
     8	  authored,
     9	  type CoreEvent,
    10	  CoreEvent as CoreEventSchema,
    11	  type CoreState,
    12	  type CorrectionAction,
    13	  Proposal as ProposalSchema,
    14	  type ProvenanceMessage,
    15	  type RelationKind,
    16	  reduce,
    17	  serializeState,
    18	} from '../src/index.js';
    19	import { ALICE, BOB } from './fixtures.js';
    20	
    21	/**
    22	 * The authority matrix, checked against an **independent oracle** rather than
    23	 * example by example.
    24	 *
    25	 * r4's delta routed this: the actor floor was pinned by hand-written cases, one
    26	 * per gate, which proves the gates that were thought of and says nothing about
    27	 * the ones that were not. Here the case space is enumerated — every event type
    28	 * against every kind of actor, and for the types that have sub-shapes (five
    29	 * object types, six correction verbs, five relation kinds, cited-or-direct
    30	 * proposals, above-or-below the confidence floor, and since r2 five shapes of
    31	 * receipt) every one of those too — and a table written from #4 and
    32	 * `authority.ts`'s doc comment says what each cell should do.
    33	 *
    34	 * **The oracle restates every rule; it never calls one.** It does not call
    35	 * `isHuman`, `actorMatchesProposer`, `MODEL_ACCEPTANCE_FLOOR`,
    36	 * `acceptanceReceiptRefusal` or `RECEIPT_POLICY` — the expectations below are
    37	 * written out from #4 and `authority.ts`'s doc comment. A shared predicate would
    38	 * make the two sides agree by construction, which is exactly the defect the
    39	 * replay suite's own oracle exists to avoid.
    40	 *
    41	 * The four values it does import from `../src` are the machinery under test and
    42	 * its plumbing — `CoreEvent` (to parse a payload), `authored` (to pair one with
    43	 * its trusted columns), `reduce` and `serializeState`. None of them encodes an
    44	 * authority decision, which is the property that matters; "imports only types"
    45	 * is what round 2 claimed here and it was never true.
    46	 *
    47	 * Two things round 2 fixed in the harness itself, both found by the r1 gauntlet:
    48	 *
    49	 *  - **The recording actor is drawn independently of the proposer.** It was
    50	 *    derived from it, so the proposal-lifecycle loop's eight cells were two
    51	 *    logs run four times each.
    52	 *  - **"Reaches every gate" walks the reducer's output**, not the oracle's. The
    53	 *    old version asked the oracle what the oracle would say, which is a
    54	 *    tautology dressed as a coverage assertion: a gate the reducer had stopped
    55	 *    firing would still have been "reached".
    56	 */
    57	
    58	const ROOM = 'room_1';
    59	const MODEL_A = 'model-a';
    60	const MODEL_B = 'model-b';
    61	
    62	/** The four kinds of actor the matrix ranges over. */
    63	type ActorKind = 'human' | 'model_proposer' | 'model_other' | 'system';
    64	const ACTOR_KINDS: ActorKind[] = ['human', 'model_proposer', 'model_other', 'system'];
    65	
    66	function actorOf(kind: ActorKind): Actor {
    67	  switch (kind) {
    68	    case 'human':
    69	      return { kind: 'human', userId: ALICE };
    70	    case 'model_proposer':
    71	      return { kind: 'model', model: MODEL_A };
    72	    case 'model_other':
    73	      return { kind: 'model', model: MODEL_B };
    74	    case 'system':
    75	      return { kind: 'system' };
    76	  }
    77	}
    78	
    79	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    80	// The oracle. Restated from the specification, not imported from the source.
    81	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    82	
    83	/**
    84	 * Every refusal the matrix can produce, and the text a room is shown for it.
    85	 *
    86	 * The markers are what makes the oracle checkable: the reducer says *why* it
    87	 * refused, and a table that only checked "something was refused" would pass on
    88	 * a reducer that refused everything for the wrong reason.
    89	 */
    90	const GATES = {
    91	  decision_acceptance: 'never auto-accepts',
    92	  commitment_acceptance: 'writes an obligation onto a named person',
    93	  objective_acceptance: 'what everything else in the room is filed under',
    94	  claim_verification: 'would become a verified claim',
    95	  direct_acceptance: 'only a human may accept an object directly',
    96	  supersession: 'retires an accepted',
    97	  answer_relation: 'declares an open question answered',
    98	  correction: 'corrections (amend, retract, restore)',
    99	  acceptance_binding: 'may only accept its own reading',
   100	  rejection_binding: 'may only withdraw its own reading',
   101	  supersession_binding: 'may only retire its own reading',
   102	  confidence_floor: 'below the floor',
   103	  payload_binding: 'does not carry its payload',
   104	  provenance_binding: 'the receipt may not change on the way through',
   105	  missing_receipt_context: 'no message window supplied',
   106	  receipt_failed: 'on a receipt that does not hold',
   107	  receipt_not_certifiable: 'on a receipt this check declines to rule on',
   108	  third_party_confirm: 'waits for the named owner to confirm',
   109	} as const;
   110	type Gate = keyof typeof GATES;
   111	
   112	/**
   113	 * θ, restated. Round 2 collapsed the reducer's floor into the engine's θ_auto,
   114	 * so these are #4's confident lines — and a type that never auto-accepts is
   115	 * unreachable rather than merely high.
   116	 */
   117	const FLOOR: Record<AcceptedObjectType, number> = {
   118	  decision: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY,
   119	  // r5: a commitment writes an obligation onto a named person and an objective
   120	  // is what everything else is filed under. Neither is a machine's to mint.
   121	  commitment: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY,
   122	  open_question: 0.6,
   123	  claim: 0.7,
   124	  objective: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY,
   125	};
   126	
   127	/** Supersession authority, restated from #4's split by what is retired. */
   128	const SUPERSESSION_NEEDS_HUMAN: Record<AcceptedObjectType, boolean> = {
   129	  decision: true,
   130	  commitment: true,
   131	  objective: true,
   132	  claim: false,
   133	  open_question: false,
   134	};
   135	
   136	const isHumanKind = (kind: ActorKind) => kind === 'human';
   137	
   138	/** "A model may act only on its own proposals; a human on any; the system on none." */
   139	function ownsProposal(kind: ActorKind, proposer: 'model_a' | 'human'): boolean {
   140	  if (kind === 'human') return true;
   141	  if (kind === 'model_proposer') return proposer === 'model_a';
   142	  return false;
   143	}
   144	
   145	interface AcceptanceCase {
   146	  kind: 'object_accepted';
   147	  actor: ActorKind;
   148	  type: AcceptedObjectType;
   149	  /** Cited proposal, or none at all (the answer-binding shape). */
   150	  cited: 'model_a' | 'human' | 'none';
   151	  /** Whether the cited proposal clears this type's floor. */
   152	  confidence: 'above' | 'below';
   153	  /** Only meaningful for a claim. */
   154	  verified: boolean;
   155	}
   156	
   157	/**
   158	 * The acceptance row of the matrix, in the order the rules bind.
   159	 *
   160	 * Order is part of the specification, not an implementation detail: a model
   161	 * accepting a decision must be told that decisions are human-only, not sent to
   162	 * fix its provenance first, because fixing the provenance would not help.
   163	 */
   164	function expectedForAcceptance(testCase: AcceptanceCase): Gate | 'allowed' {
   165	  const human = isHumanKind(testCase.actor);
   166	  if (testCase.type === 'decision' && !human) return 'decision_acceptance';
   167	  if (testCase.type === 'commitment' && !human) return 'commitment_acceptance';
   168	  if (testCase.type === 'objective' && !human) return 'objective_acceptance';
   169	  if (testCase.type === 'claim' && testCase.verified && !human) return 'claim_verification';
   170	  if (testCase.cited === 'none' && !human) return 'direct_acceptance';
   171	  if (testCase.cited !== 'none' && !ownsProposal(testCase.actor, testCase.cited)) {
   172	    return 'acceptance_binding';
   173	  }
   174	  if (testCase.cited !== 'none' && !human && testCase.confidence === 'below') {
   175	    return 'confidence_floor';
   176	  }
   177	  return 'allowed';
   178	}
   179	
   180	/** The shapes a receipt can take on the way through acceptance. */
   181	type ReceiptShape =
   182	  | 'faithful'
   183	  | 'payload'
   184	  | 'citations'
   185	  | 'no_window'
   186	  | 'wrong_author'
   187	  | 'uncertifiable';
   188	const RECEIPT_SHAPES: ReceiptShape[] = [
   189	  'faithful',
   190	  'payload',
   191	  'citations',
   192	  'no_window',
   193	  'wrong_author',
   194	  // r5: the quote contains every word of the statement, in order, and says
   195	  // more. `receipt_not_certifiable` was declared as a gate in r4 and no case in
   196	  // this matrix reached it, so the reducer could have stopped enforcing it
   197	  // without a test noticing.
   198	  'uncertifiable',
   199	];
   200	
   201	/**
   202	 * The receipt row, added in round 2 — every one of these was a call-site manner
   203	 * before, and a caller that skipped it got an auto-acceptance.
   204	 *
   205	 * A human runs none of it: a person accepting a reading has read it.
   206	 */
   207	function expectedForReceipt(actor: ActorKind, shape: ReceiptShape): Gate | 'allowed' {
   208	  if (!ownsProposal(actor, 'model_a')) return 'acceptance_binding';
   209	  if (isHumanKind(actor)) return 'allowed';
   210	  switch (shape) {
   211	    case 'payload':
   212	      return 'payload_binding';
   213	    case 'citations':
   214	      return 'provenance_binding';
   215	    case 'no_window':
   216	      return 'missing_receipt_context';
   217	    case 'wrong_author':
   218	      return 'receipt_failed';
   219	    case 'uncertifiable':
   220	      return 'receipt_not_certifiable';
   221	    case 'faithful':
   222	      return 'allowed';
   223	  }
   224	}
   225	
   226	function expectedForCorrection(actor: ActorKind): Gate | 'allowed' {
   227	  return isHumanKind(actor) ? 'allowed' : 'correction';
   228	}
   229	
   230	function expectedForRelation(
   231	  actor: ActorKind,
   232	  kind: RelationKind,
   233	  retires: AcceptedObjectType | null,
   234	): Gate | 'allowed' {
   235	  if (kind === 'answers' && !isHumanKind(actor)) return 'answer_relation';
   236	  if (
   237	    kind === 'supersedes' &&
   238	    retires !== null &&
   239	    SUPERSESSION_NEEDS_HUMAN[retires] &&
   240	    !isHumanKind(actor)
   241	  ) {
   242	    return 'supersession';
   243	  }
   244	  return 'allowed';
   245	}
   470	    : { outcome: 'applied', event, actor };
   471	}
   472	
   473	/** Applies one event. Returns whether it was applied; issues are recorded in state. */
   474	function dispatch(state: CoreState, entry: AuthoredEvent): boolean {
   475	  const { event, actor } = entry;
   476	  switch (event.type) {
   477	    case 'proposal_recorded':
   478	      return applyProposalRecorded(state, event);
   479	    case 'proposal_rejected':
   480	      return applyProposalRejected(state, event, actor);
   481	    case 'proposal_superseded':
   482	      return applyProposalSuperseded(state, event, actor);
   483	    case 'object_accepted':
   484	      return applyObjectAccepted(state, event, actor, entry.messages);
   485	    case 'object_corrected':
   486	      return applyObjectCorrected(state, event, actor);
   487	    case 'relation_added':
   488	      return applyRelationAdded(state, event, actor);
   489	    default: {
   490	      const exhaustive: never = event;
   491	      fail(state, (exhaustive as CoreEvent).id, 'unknown event type');
   492	      return false;
   493	    }
   494	  }
   495	}
   496	
   497	type EventOf<T extends CoreEvent['type']> = Extract<CoreEvent, { type: T }>;
   498	
   499	function applyProposalRecorded(state: CoreState, event: EventOf<'proposal_recorded'>): boolean {
   500	  const { proposal } = event;
   501	  if (state.proposals[proposal.id]) {
   502	    fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${proposal.id}" already recorded`);
   503	    return false;
   504	  }
   505	
   506	  // A proposal enters the log as a proposal, full stop. An incoming status of
   507	  // `accepted` is an interpreter asserting that its own reading is a fact —
   508	  // exactly what the acceptance boundary exists to refuse (init.md §5). The
   509	  // status is forced back to `proposed` and the coercion is recorded, so the
   510	  // proposal is still staged for a human but can never arrive pre-blessed.
   511	  if (proposal.status !== 'proposed') {
   512	    fail(
   513	      state,
   514	      event.id,
   515	      `proposal "${proposal.id}" was recorded with status "${proposal.status}" — forced to "proposed"; only an acceptance or rejection event may move a proposal`,
   516	    );
   517	  }
   518	
   519	  // The wire status is dropped rather than copied: `record.status` is the only
   520	  // status a proposal has, so acceptance cannot leave a stale second copy
   521	  // behind. See `StoredProposal`.
   522	  state.proposals[proposal.id] = {
   523	    proposal: storeProposal(proposal),
   524	    status: 'proposed',
   525	    acceptedObjectId: null,
   526	    rejectedReason: null,
   527	    supersededByProposalId: null,
   528	    supersededReason: null,
   529	  };
   530	  return true;
   531	}
   532	
   533	function applyProposalRejected(
   534	  state: CoreState,
   535	  event: EventOf<'proposal_rejected'>,
   536	  actor: Actor,
   537	): boolean {
   538	  const record = state.proposals[event.proposalId];
   539	  if (!record) {
   540	    fail(state, event.id, `unknown proposal "${event.proposalId}"`);
   541	    return false;
   542	  }
   543	
   544	  // Actor binding, before status — authority is a property of the event, status
   545	  // is a property of what it points at, and an interpreter with no business
   546	  // touching this proposal should be told that rather than sent to check
   547	  // whether it is still open.
   548	  //
   549	  // r4 left `proposal_rejected` open to any actor on the grounds that
   550	  // withdrawing a staged reading destroys nothing. That is true of a model
   551	  // withdrawing *its own* reading. It is not true of one interpreter retiring
   552	  // another's — or a human's — before anyone has seen it, which is a silent
   553	  // delete with no correction chain, performed by the one kind of actor that may
   554	  // not correct anything.
   555	  if (!actorMatchesProposer(actor, record.proposal.proposer)) {
   556	    fail(
   557	      state,
   558	      event.id,
   559	      proposalBindingRefusal(
   560	        'rejection_binding',
   561	        actor,
   562	        record.proposal.proposer,
   563	        event.proposalId,
   564	      ),
   565	    );
   566	    return false;
   567	  }
   568	
   569	  if (record.status === 'accepted') {
   570	    fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${event.proposalId}" was already accepted`);
   571	    return false;
   572	  }
   573	  if (record.status === 'rejected') {
   574	    fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${event.proposalId}" was already rejected`);
   575	    return false;
   576	  }
   577	  if (record.status === 'superseded') {
   578	    fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${event.proposalId}" was already superseded`);
   579	    return false;
   580	  }
   581	  record.status = 'rejected';
   582	  record.rejectedReason = event.reason;
   583	  return true;
   584	}
   585	
   586	/**
   587	 * A newer reading retires an older one. See `ProposalSuperseded` for why this is
   588	 * not a rejection: nobody judged it, so telling the room a person declined it
   589	 * would be a lie about who did what.
   590	 */
   591	function applyProposalSuperseded(
   592	  state: CoreState,
   593	  event: EventOf<'proposal_superseded'>,
   594	  actor: Actor,
   595	): boolean {
   596	  const record = state.proposals[event.proposalId];
   597	  if (!record) {
   598	    fail(state, event.id, `unknown proposal "${event.proposalId}"`);
   599	    return false;
   600	  }
   601	  if (!actorMatchesProposer(actor, record.proposal.proposer)) {
   602	    fail(
   603	      state,
   604	      event.id,
   605	      proposalBindingRefusal(
   606	        'supersession_binding',
   607	        actor,
   608	        record.proposal.proposer,
   609	        event.proposalId,
   610	      ),
   611	    );
   612	    return false;
   613	  }
   614	  if (record.status !== 'proposed') {
   615	    fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${event.proposalId}" was already ${record.status}`);
   616	    return false;
   617	  }
   618	
   619	  const byId = event.supersededByProposalId;
   620	  if (byId !== null) {
   621	    if (byId === event.proposalId) {
   622	      fail(state, event.id, `proposal "${event.proposalId}" cannot supersede itself`);
   623	      return false;
   624	    }
   625	    const replacement = state.proposals[byId];
   626	    if (!replacement) {
   627	      fail(
   628	        state,
   629	        event.id,
   630	        `proposal "${event.proposalId}" is superseded by unknown proposal "${byId}"`,
   631	      );
   632	      return false;
   633	    }
   634	    if (replacement.proposal.roomId !== record.proposal.roomId) {
   635	      fail(
   636	        state,
   637	        event.id,
   638	        `proposal "${byId}" is in room "${replacement.proposal.roomId}", not "${record.proposal.roomId}" — a re-reading cannot cross rooms`,
   639	      );
   640	      return false;
   641	    }
   642	  }
   643	
   644	  record.status = 'superseded';
   645	  record.supersededByProposalId = byId;
   646	  record.supersededReason = event.reason;
   647	  return true;
   648	}
   649	
   650	function applyObjectAccepted(
   651	  state: CoreState,
   652	  event: EventOf<'object_accepted'>,
   653	  actor: Actor,
   654	  messages: TrustedContext['messages'],
   655	): boolean {
   656	  const { object } = event;
   657	  if (state.objects[object.id]) {
   658	    fail(state, event.id, `object "${object.id}" already accepted`);
   659	    return false;
   660	  }

   226	    );
   227	    expect(normalizeForReceipt('ﬁle_handle')).not.toBe(normalizeForReceipt('file_handle'));
   228	  });
   229	});
   230	
   231	describe('r5 — a later correction is part of the receipt', () => {
   232	  const messages: ProvenanceMessage[] = [
   233	    { id: 'msg_1', authorId: ALICE, body: 'We will deploy production Friday.' },
   234	    { id: 'msg_2', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Correction: we will not deploy production Friday.' },
   235	  ];
   236	  const STATEMENT = 'We will deploy production Friday.';
   237	
   238	  it('refuses to certify a sentence a later message in the window corrects', () => {
   239	    expect(
   240	      kinds(
   241	        {
   242	          type: 'claim',
   243	          provenance: ['msg_1'],
   244	          quote: STATEMENT,
   245	          statement: STATEMENT,
   246	          proposer: { kind: 'model' },
   247	          attributedTo: ALICE,
   248	        },
   249	        messages,
   250	      ),
   251	    ).toEqual(['superseded_by_later_message']);
   252	  });
   253	
   254	  it('does not auto-accept it at the engine', () => {
   255	    const decision = decideAcceptance(
   256	      modelProposal({
   257	        type: 'claim',
   258	        payload: { statement: STATEMENT, claimant: ALICE },
   259	        quote: STATEMENT,
   260	      }),
   261	      { messages },
   262	    );
   263	    expect(decision.rule).toBe('receipt_not_certifiable');
   264	  });
   265	
   266	  it('leaves an unrelated later message alone', () => {
   267	    expect(
   268	      kinds(
   269	        {
   270	          type: 'claim',
   271	          provenance: ['msg_1'],
   272	          quote: STATEMENT,
   273	          statement: STATEMENT,
   274	          proposer: { kind: 'model' },
   275	          attributedTo: ALICE,
   276	        },
   277	        [
   278	          messages[0] as ProvenanceMessage,
   279	          { id: 'msg_2', authorId: BOB, body: 'The staging cluster is green.' },
   280	        ],
   281	      ),
   282	    ).toEqual([]);
   283	  });
   284	});
   285	
   286	describe('r5 — speech-act fitness', () => {
   287	  it('does not mint a question as a claim', () => {
   288	    const messages: ProvenanceMessage[] = [
   289	      { id: 'msg_1', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Would we deploy production Friday?' },
   290	    ];
   291	    expect(
   292	      kinds(
   293	        {
   294	          type: 'claim',
   295	          provenance: ['msg_1'],
   296	          quote: 'Would we deploy production Friday?',
   297	          statement: 'Would we deploy production Friday?',
   298	          proposer: { kind: 'model' },
   299	          attributedTo: ALICE,
   300	        },
   301	        messages,
   302	      ),
   303	    ).toEqual(['statement_is_not_an_assertion']);
   304	  });
   305	
   306	  it('mints the same sentence as an open question', () => {
   307	    const messages: ProvenanceMessage[] = [
   308	      { id: 'msg_1', authorId: ALICE, body: 'Would we deploy production Friday?' },
   309	    ];
   310	    expect(
   311	      kinds(
   312	        {
   313	          type: 'open_question',
   314	          provenance: ['msg_1'],
   315	          quote: 'Would we deploy production Friday?',
    // this function constructed and inspected only the *cited* messages — so a
    // window holding "We will deploy production Friday." followed by
    // "Correction: we will not deploy production Friday.", citing only the
    // first, was a clean receipt. The message table being append-only stops the
    // first message being edited; it does not make the second part of
    // acceptance.
    //
    // Deliberately not a marker list ("correction", "actually", `s/x/y/`) —
    // those route which model reads a window and have no business deciding
    // whether a reading becomes a fact. The test is structural and reuses the
    // alignment: a later message **restates the statement and adds to it**. That
    // catches the inserted `not`, the added `unless`, and the retraction verb,
    // without an opinion about which words are dangerous. An exact restatement
    // is not a correction and a message about something else does not align at
    // all.
    if (fromModel && cited.length > 0) {
      const revisited = laterRevision(
        subject.statement ?? '',
        subject.provenance,
        messages,
        policy,
      );
      if (revisited) {
        problems.push({
          kind: 'superseded_by_later_message',
          severity: 'refer',
          detail: `message "${revisited.message.id}" comes after every message this cites and says the same sentence with ${revisited.added.map((token) => `"${token}"`).join(', ')} added — a later message in the same window revisits the one being quoted, and whether that reverses it, narrows it or merely repeats it is not something a machine may decide from the words`,
          messageId: revisited.message.id,
        });
      }
    }

    // ── …and it is being offered as an assertion at all ────────────────────
    //
    // r5. `objects.ts` requires a nonempty string and the receipt proves string
    // equality, so `"Would we deploy production Friday?"` minted as a `claim`

    27	/**
    28	 * #4's acceptance matrix, one test per cell.
    29	 *
    30	 * The table under test, from `decideAcceptance`'s own doc comment:
    31	 *
    32	 * | type                     | c < θ_min | θ_min ≤ c < θ_auto | c ≥ θ_auto             |
    33	 * | ------------------------ | --------- | ------------------ | ---------------------- |
    34	 * | claim                    | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept            |
    35	 * | open_question            | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept            |
    36	 * | objective                | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept            |
    37	 * | commitment, self-stated  | discard   | pending, quiet     | auto-accept            |
    38	 * | commitment, third-party  | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, owner confirm |
    39	 * | decision                 | discard   | pending, quiet     | pending, Needs-you     |
    40	 *
    41	 * Eighteen cells, driven from a table rather than written out, so a cell cannot
    42	 * be quietly dropped: the count is asserted at the end.

    45	/**
    46	 * Every sentence the fixtures quote — **one per message**.
    47	 *
    48	 * r5 moved these out of a single five-sentence body. A certifiable quote is now
    49	 * the whole of what its author wrote in the bearing message (see
    50	 * `quoteCoversOwnText`), because a neighbouring sentence can reverse the one
    51	 * being quoted and no rule about the quoted span can see that. Five sentences
    52	 * jammed into one body was a test convenience that no longer describes any
    53	 * message anybody sends, and keeping it would have meant every cell of #4's
    54	 * matrix exercising the referral path instead of the cell it names.
    55	 */

    57	  decision: 'Reset narrowing on mutating method calls.',

    59	  open_question: 'Do we keep the flag after launch?',

    89	function proposal(overrides: {
    90	  type: AcceptedObjectType;
    91	  confidence: number;
    92	  payload?: Record<string, unknown>;
    93	  proposer?: Proposal['proposer'];
    94	  quote?: string | null;
    95	}): Proposal {
    96	  const payload =
    97	    overrides.payload ??
    98	    ({
    99	      // r4: each statement is the sentence its `QUOTE` carries, word for word.
   100	      // The receipt no longer measures how many words two texts share; it asks
   101	      // whether the statement is the quote with nothing dropped but articles, so
   102	      // "Land the migration" quoted against "I'll land the migration tomorrow"
   103	      // is a reading a person has to confirm rather than one a model may accept.
   104	      decision: { statement: 'Reset narrowing on mutating method calls' },
   105	      commitment: { statement: "I'll land the migration tomorrow", owner: ALICE },
   106	      open_question: { question: 'Do we keep the flag after launch?' },
   107	      claim: { statement: 'The migration is reversible', claimant: ALICE },
   108	      objective: { title: 'Ship the narrowing fix this quarter' },
   109	    }[overrides.type] as Record<string, unknown>);
   110	
   111	  return ProposalSchema.parse({
   112	    id: 'prop_matrix',
   113	    roomId: ROOM,
   114	    type: overrides.type,
   115	    payload,
   116	    confidence: overrides.confidence,
   117	    proposer: overrides.proposer ?? { kind: 'model', model: 'test-model' },
   118	    provenance: [MESSAGE_ID[overrides.type]],
   119	    quote: overrides.quote === undefined ? QUOTE[overrides.type] : overrides.quote,
   120	    createdAt: at(1),
   121	  });
   122	}

   124	interface Cell {
   125	  label: string;
   126	  type: AcceptedObjectType;
   127	  band: 'below' | 'between' | 'above';
   128	  attribution?: 'self' | 'third_party';
   129	  verdict: AcceptanceVerdict;
   130	  visibility: AcceptanceVisibility;
   131	  rule: AcceptanceRuleName;
   132	  awaitingConfirmFrom?: string | null;
   133	}
   134	
   135	/**
   136	 * A confidence squarely inside each band — **written out, not derived**.
   137	 *
   138	 * Round 2's gauntlet kept finding the same shape here, and it kept being right:
   139	 * these numbers used to be computed from `DEFAULT_ACCEPTANCE_RULES`, which makes
   140	 * the whole matrix invariant under a change to the table. Move θ_auto for a claim
   141	 * from 0.7 to 0.6 and every probe moved with it, so the suite proved the *rules*
   142	 * and said nothing about the *thresholds*. r1 patched that with one pin-by-value
   143	 * test; r2's delta found the derivation still here and the single pin still
   144	 * carrying all of it alone.
   145	 *
   146	 * So the probes are literals, and `pinsMatchTheTable` below checks — from the
   147	 * table, once, in one place — that each literal really is in the band its label
   148	 * claims. A θ change now breaks the probe *and* the pin, and both say why.
   149	 */
   150	const PROBE: Record<AcceptedObjectType, Record<Cell['band'], number>> = {
   151	  //                 below θ_min   in the band   at or above θ_auto
   152	  decision: { below: 0.4, between: 0.6, above: 0.7 },
   153	  commitment: { below: 0.4, between: 0.62, above: 0.75 },
   154	  open_question: { below: 0.3, between: 0.5, above: 0.6 },
   155	  claim: { below: 0.4, between: 0.6, above: 0.7 },
   156	  objective: { below: 0.4, between: 0.62, above: 0.75 },
   157	};
   158	
   159	function confidenceFor(type: AcceptedObjectType, band: Cell['band']): number {
   160	  return PROBE[type][band];
   161	}
   162	
   163	const CELLS: Cell[] = [];
   164	for (const type of ['claim', 'open_question'] as const) {
   165	  CELLS.push(
   166	    {
   167	      label: `${type} below θ_min`,
   168	      type,
   169	      band: 'below',
   170	      verdict: 'discard',
   171	      visibility: 'none',
   172	      rule: 'below_theta_min',
   173	    },
   174	    {
   175	      label: `${type} in the θ band`,
   176	      type,
   177	      band: 'between',
   178	      verdict: 'pending',
   179	      visibility: 'quiet',
   180	      rule: 'theta_band',
   181	    },
   182	    {
   183	      label: `${type} at θ_auto`,
   184	      type,
   185	      band: 'above',
   186	      verdict: 'auto_accept',
   187	      visibility: 'accepted',
   188	      rule: 'auto_accept',
   189	    },
   190	  );
   191	}
   192	for (const band of ['below', 'between', 'above'] as const) {
   193	  CELLS.push({
   194	    label: `objective ${{ below: 'below θ_min', between: 'in the θ band', above: 'at θ_auto' }[band]}`,
   195	    type: 'objective',
   196	    band,
   197	    verdict: band === 'below' ? 'discard' : 'pending',
   198	    visibility: band === 'below' ? 'none' : band === 'between' ? 'quiet' : 'needs_you',
   199	    rule:
   200	      band === 'below'
   201	        ? 'below_theta_min'
   202	        : band === 'between'
   203	          ? 'theta_band'
   204	          : 'never_auto_accepts',
   205	  });
   206	}
   207	for (const attribution of ['self', 'third_party'] as const) {

   214	      verdict: 'discard',

   227	  );
   228	}
   229	CELLS.push(

   233	    band: 'above',
   234	    attribution: 'self',

   236	    // not. #4's row split on who the sentence is *about*; it was silent on who
   237	    // does the accepting, and #44's fact-check drove a model straight through
   238	    // that silence to mint an obligation naming a person.
   239	    verdict: 'pending',
   240	    visibility: 'needs_you',
   241	    rule: 'never_auto_accepts',
   242	  },
   243	  {
   244	    label: 'commitment (third-party) at θ_auto',

   253	  {

   287	describe('#4 acceptance matrix — one test per cell', () => {
   288	  for (const cell of CELLS) {
   289	    it(cell.label, () => {
   290	      const confidence = cell.label.endsWith('confidence 1.0')
   291	        ? 1
   292	        : confidenceFor(cell.type, cell.band);
   293	      // Attribution is decided by who wrote the message bearing the sentence, so
   294	      // a third-party cell is the same proposal read against a window somebody
   295	      // else wrote. Every other cell uses the window whose author matches the
   296	      // person named, because a mismatched one is a *receipt* failure and that
   297	      // has its own tests.
   298	      const messages = cell.attribution === 'third_party' ? bobMessages : aliceMessages;
   299	      const decision = decideAcceptance(proposal({ type: cell.type, confidence }), { messages });
   300	
   301	      expect(decision.verdict).toBe(cell.verdict);
   302	      expect(decision.visibility).toBe(cell.visibility);
   303	      expect(decision.rule).toBe(cell.rule);
   304	      expect(decision.awaitingConfirmFrom).toBe(cell.awaitingConfirmFrom ?? null);
   305	      // Every decision explains itself: the reason is what a room is shown.
   306	      expect(decision.reason.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   307	      // A discard short-circuits before attribution is computed, on purpose:
   308	      // there is nobody to ask about a reading nobody will see.
   309	      if (cell.attribution && cell.verdict !== 'discard') {
   310	        expect(decision.attribution).toBe(cell.attribution);
   311	      }
   312	    });

     2	import {
     3	  bearingMessage,
     4	  type ProvenanceMessage,
     5	  type ProvenanceProblem,
     6	  referringProblems,
     7	  rejectingProblems,
     8	  validateProposalProvenance,
     9	} from './escalation.js';
    10	import { type AuthoredEvent, authored, type CoreEvent, trustedContext } from './events.js';
    11	import { alignTokens, hasContent, routingTokens } from './matching.js';
    12	import {
    13	  type AcceptedObjectType,
    14	  type ClaimPayload,
    15	  type DecisionPayload,
    16	  payloadText,
    17	} from './objects.js';

    23	/**
    24	 * The acceptance engine — #4's matrix, entire.
    25	 *
    26	 * The reducer holds the *floor* of this matrix (`authority.ts`): the rows that
    27	 * are trust boundaries, enforced where nothing can route around them. This file
    28	 * decides what a worker should emit at all, given inputs the reducer applies
    29	 * more narrowly — the messages a reading was drawn from, who wrote them, what
    30	 * the room has already accepted.
    31	 *
    32	 * The two read one θ table (`policy.ts`), so they cannot disagree about whether
    33	 * a reading is strong enough, and `AcceptanceConfig` refuses to be configured
    34	 * below the floor, so the engine can only ever be the stricter of the two.
    35	 *
    36	 * ## What "two boundaries" does and does not mean
    37	 *
    38	 * r4's receipt said the engine and the reducer are two boundaries, unqualified,
    39	 * and r4's blind review was right that the claim outruns the code. Both call the
    40	 * *same* `validateProposalProvenance`, so **every defect in quote semantics
    41	 * survives both**: switching off the engine does not help, because the reducer
    42	 * accepts what the validator passes; switching off the reducer does not help,
    43	 * because the engine emits `auto_accept` on the same input.
    44	 *
    45	 * That is deliberate and it stays. Two implementations of "does this quote bear
    46	 * this sentence" is two answers to one question, which is the defect
    47	 * `policy.ts` exists to prevent for θ — and `RETRO.md` records what it costs
    48	 * when a rule has two homes. So the claim is scoped instead of withdrawn or
    49	 * padded:
    50	 *
    51	 *  - **Independently enforced at the floor** (`authority.ts`, and nothing here
    52	 *    can weaken them): who the actor is, that a proposal is cited and is real,
    53	 *    open, type-matching and room-matching, that a non-human acted on its own
    54	 *    reading, the confidence floor, payload equality, provenance equality, that
    55	 *    a message window exists at all, and that a quote exists at all.
    56	 *  - **One implementation, called from both**: everything about what the quote
    57	 *    *means* — where it sits in its message, what the statement drops or adds,
    58	 *    who wrote it, and whether the window revisits it. A defect here is a defect
    59	 *    in both layers, and `boundary.test.ts` pins that as a property rather than
    60	 *    letting a receipt imply otherwise.
    61	 *
    62	 * **The receipt is not optional, and an empty one is not a receipt.** In round 1
    63	 * `messages` was an optional field whose absence produced an empty problem set,
    64	 * so the caller that forgot it got auto-acceptance instead of a refusal — the
    65	 * exact fail-open shape a trust boundary must not have. Round 2 made it required
    66	 * and round 2's gauntlet walked through the door that was left: `messages: []`
    67	 * satisfies "required" and satisfies nothing else. A model proposal judged with
    68	 * no window *or an empty one* is discarded with `missing_message_context`.
    69	 *
    70	 * Everything here is pure. No clock: `answerBinding` takes its timestamp. No id
    71	 * generation: it takes its ids. That is what makes an acceptance decision
    72	 * reproducible from the log months later, which is the property the whole
    73	 * correction story rests on.
    74	 */
    75	
    76	/* ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    77	 * The decision
    78	 * ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
    79	
    80	/** Who a commitment's sentence came from. */
    81	export type CommitmentAttribution =
    82	  /** The owner wrote the message bearing it — "I'll finish it tomorrow". */
    83	  | 'self'
    84	  /** Somebody else did — "Justin will handle it". Nobody gets committed by it. */
    85	  | 'third_party';
    86	
    87	export type AcceptanceVerdict =
    88	  /** Accept it now, as `~`. */
    89	  | 'auto_accept'
    90	  /** Keep it staged. `visibility` says where it shows. */
    91	  | 'pending'
    92	  /** Drop it. Never shown, never stored as a live proposal. */
    93	  | 'discard';
    94	
    95	export type AcceptanceVisibility =
    96	  /** It became an object; nothing is pending. */
    97	  | 'accepted'
    98	  /** Current state, marked unconfirmed. Never in Needs-you. */
    99	  | 'quiet'
   100	  /** Needs-you: somebody has to act on it. */
   101	  | 'needs_you'
   102	  /** Nowhere. */
   103	  | 'none';
   104	
   105	/**
   106	 * Which cell of the matrix fired. One name per cell, so tests can pin them.
   107	 *
   108	 * **Data, so a coverage test can iterate it instead of restating it.** r4's
   109	 * blind review found the shape this closes: `acceptance.test.ts` carried a test
   110	 * titled *"reaches every rule name the type declares"* whose supposedly complete
   111	 * list omitted `receipt_not_certifiable`, so the test passed and would have gone
   112	 * on passing if that engine behaviour were deleted. A hand-written list catches
   113	 * a rule that disappears from the source and is blind to one that appears; the
   114	 * derived list is blind the other way. Deriving the *type* from the data makes
   115	 * them the same object, so a test iterating it is exhaustive by construction.
   116	 *
   117	 *  - `missing_message_context` — no window, an empty one, or one with no words
   118	 *    in any body. A model reading judged without the messages it cites.
   119	 *  - `provenance_failed` — a `reject`-severity receipt problem.
   120	 *  - `receipt_not_certifiable` — a `refer`-severity one: the check declines to
   121	 *    rule either way, so it is never auto-accepted and never discarded.
   122	 *  - `duplicate_of_accepted` — the room already accepted this, from these
   123	 *    messages.
   124	 *  - `below_theta_min` — too weak to be worth anybody's attention, with nothing
   125	 *    wrong in the receipt.
   126	 *  - `theta_band` — between θ_min and θ_auto: shown quietly, never Needs-you.
   127	 *  - `auto_accept` — at or above θ_auto, for a type that may auto-accept.
   128	 *  - `never_auto_accepts` — at or above θ_auto, for a type that never does at
   129	 *    any confidence. Named for the *rule* rather than the type: it was
   130	 *    `decision_never_auto`, which misnamed every non-decision that reached it,
   131	 *    and since r5 three types are in that row.
   132	 *  - `third_party_commitment` — an obligation somebody else's sentence put a
   133	 *    name on. It goes to the named owner.
   134	 *  - `human_proposer` — a person staged it, so θ does not apply.
   135	 */
   136	export const ACCEPTANCE_RULE_NAMES = [
   137	  'missing_message_context',
   138	  'provenance_failed',
   139	  'receipt_not_certifiable',
   140	  'duplicate_of_accepted',
   141	  'below_theta_min',
   142	  'theta_band',
   143	  'auto_accept',
   144	  'never_auto_accepts',
   145	  'third_party_commitment',
   146	  'human_proposer',
   147	] as const;
   148	
   149	export type AcceptanceRuleName = (typeof ACCEPTANCE_RULE_NAMES)[number];
   150	
   151	export interface AcceptanceDecision {
   152	  verdict: AcceptanceVerdict;
   153	  visibility: AcceptanceVisibility;
   154	  /** The named owner who must confirm, for a third-party commitment. */
   155	  awaitingConfirmFrom: Id | null;
   156	  /** The matrix cell. */
   157	  rule: AcceptanceRuleName;
   158	  /** Why, in words a room could be shown. */
   159	  reason: string;
   160	  /** The confident line for this type. */
   161	  thetaAuto: number;
   162	  /** Whether crossing it may accept, or only surface. */
   163	  autoAcceptAvailable: boolean;
   164	  thetaMin: number;
   165	  /** Only set for commitments. */
   166	  attribution: CommitmentAttribution | null;
   167	  /** The accepted object this duplicates, when that is why it was discarded. */
   168	  duplicateOf: Id | null;
   169	}
   170	
   171	/** An already-accepted object, as the deduplicator needs it. */
   172	export interface AcceptedObjectRef {
   173	  objectId: Id;
   174	  type: AcceptedObjectType;
   175	  /** `statement` / `question` / `title`, whichever the type carries. */
   176	  text: string;
   177	  /** The messages it was drawn from. */
   178	  messageIds: readonly Id[];
   179	}
   180	
   181	export interface AcceptanceContext {
   182	  config?: AcceptanceConfig;
   183	  /**
   184	   * The window's messages — **required, and non-empty**, and the reason is the
   185	   * whole of round 1's second blocking finding. Commitment attribution and
   186	   * provenance validation cannot be done without them, and in round 1 that meant
   187	   * they silently were not done: no messages, no problems, auto-accept. A model
   188	   * proposal judged with no window is discarded now.
   189	   *
   190	   * The type cannot say "non-empty", so the check does: `[]` is refused with the
   191	   * same rule as `undefined`. Round 2's gauntlet found the version that only
   192	   * asked about `undefined`, and an empty array is not a smaller window — it is
   193	   * the same absence with a different spelling.
   194	   *
   195	   * A human-staged proposal does not go through θ at all, so it survives an
   196	   * empty window; there is nothing to check when the person staging the reading
   197	   * is the receipt.
   198	   */
   199	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[];
   200	  /**
   201	   * What the room has already accepted, for deduplication.
   202	   *
   203	   * This is where dedup belongs. The spike tested the alternative directly —
   204	   * putting a compressed accepted-state block in the extraction prompt — and it
   205	   * dedups well (zero re-proposed objects) at a cost of collapsing recall from
   206	   * 19 objects to 11, losing the dispute edge and the `disputed` flag, and
   207	   * producing no cross-window relations at all. It is a de-duplication input,
   208	   * not a comprehension aid. Doing it here costs nothing and is testable
   209	   * without a model.
   210	   */
   211	  acceptedObjects?: readonly AcceptedObjectRef[];
   212	}
   213	
   214	/** The person a payload puts on the hook, if any. */
   215	export function payloadAttributedTo(
   216	  type: AcceptedObjectType,
   217	  payload: Record<string, unknown>,
   218	): Id | null {
   219	  const key = type === 'claim' ? 'claimant' : type === 'commitment' ? 'owner' : null;
   220	  if (key === null) return null;
   221	  const value = payload[key];
   222	  return typeof value === 'string' ? value : null;
   223	}
   224	
   225	/**
   226	 * Did the owner of this commitment write the message it was read out of?
   227	 *
   228	 * **The message bearing the sentence, not any cited message.** Round 1's
   229	 * gauntlet: an owner who authored *any* cited message counted as a
   230	 * self-statement, so padding `provenance` with one unrelated message the owner
   231	 * happened to write turned "Justin will handle it" into "Justin said he would
   232	 * handle it" and auto-accepted an obligation nobody agreed to. The quote names
   233	 * the bearing message; its author is the only authorship that answers the
   234	 * question.
   235	 *
   236	 * With no quote, or no window, the answer is `third_party`, deliberately. #4's
   237	 * rule is "nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence", and the only way
   238	 * to honour it without evidence is to ask the named person. An unproven
   239	 * self-statement is a third-party statement.
   240	 */
   241	export function commitmentAttribution(
   242	  owner: Id,
   243	  citedMessageIds: readonly Id[],
   244	  messages: readonly ProvenanceMessage[] | undefined,
   245	  quote: string | null | undefined,
   246	): CommitmentAttribution {
   247	  // Absent, empty, and empty-of-content are one state: nobody supplied the words.
   248	  if (!messages?.some((message) => hasContent(message.body))) return 'third_party';
   249	  if (!hasContent(quote)) return 'third_party';
   250	  const cited = new Set(citedMessageIds);
   251	  const citedMessages = messages.filter((message) => cited.has(message.id));
   252	  const bearing = bearingMessage(quote, citedMessages);
   253	  return bearing !== null && bearing.authorId === owner ? 'self' : 'third_party';
   254	}
   255	
   256	/**
   257	 * Is this reading something the room already accepted?
   258	 *
   259	 * Same sentence **and** provenance overlap, both required. Either alone is a bad
   260	 * matcher: two different decisions drawn from one message share provenance
   261	 * completely, and two readings of one subsystem share most of their words.
   262	 *
   263	 * ## Why this is not a similarity score any more
   264	 *
   265	 * **r5, from the audit of what every documented limit does with an input inside
   145	 * destination is then a statement that drops words the quote carries, which is
   146	 * the case `refer` exists for: a person looks at where the link actually goes.
   147	 */
   148	const MARKDOWN_LINK = /!?\[([^\]]*)\]\(\s*([^)\s]*)(?:\s+"[^"]*")?\s*\)/g;
   149	
   150	/**
   151	 * Code, in every spelling Markdown gives it: a fence, a double-backtick span, a
   152	 * single-backtick span.
   153	 *
   154	 * Split on rather than folded away, because the prose rules above are wrong
   155	 * inside code — `*` is a glob, `_` is an identifier, `[a](b)` is a literal — and
   156	 * because **the backticks themselves are content**. A backticked span is
   157	 * *mention, not use*: `` `Deploy production Friday.` `` is a string its author
   158	 * displayed, not a sentence its author asserted, and r4's normalization deleted
   159	 * exactly the two characters that carry that distinction. `~~` was excluded from
   160	 * folding in r4 for the same class of reason (strikethrough is retraction, not
   161	 * emphasis); this is that argument applied to the delimiter that separates
   162	 * quoting a thing from saying it.
   163	 */
   164	const CODE_SPAN = /(```[\s\S]*?```|``[\s\S]*?``|`[^`\n]*`)/;
   165	
   166	/**
   167	 * The differences a quote may have from the text it was taken from — **and no
   168	 * others.**
   169	 *
   170	 * In order, with the argument that admits each:
   171	 *
   172	 *  1. **Whitespace runs collapse, and the ends are trimmed.** A client wraps a
   173	 *     message and a quote copied out of it carries different line breaks. A run
   174	 *     of horizontal space is not a token in any script.
   175	 *  2. **Characters with no rendering are dropped.** See `INVISIBLE`.
   176	 *  3. **A typographic apostrophe is an apostrophe.** `won’t` and `won't` are one
   177	 *     word entered two ways; treating them as two produces a refusal that has
   178	 *     nothing to do with what was said.
   179	 *  4. **Paired asterisk emphasis may be absent.** See `PAIRED_EMPHASIS`.
   180	 *  5. **A link contributes its destination.** See `MARKDOWN_LINK`.
   181	 *
   182	 * Not admitted, and each one used to be: NFKC compatibility folding, case
   183	 * folding, backtick deletion, underscore emphasis, bare link-text substitution.
   184	 * The header of this file says why.
   185	 */
   186	export function normalizeForReceipt(text: string): string {
   187	  const out: string[] = [];
   188	  for (const [index, segment] of text.split(CODE_SPAN).entries()) {
   189	    // `String.split` with one capture group alternates: prose, delimiter, prose…
   190	    const isCode = index % 2 === 1;
   191	    out.push(isCode ? segment.replace(INVISIBLE, '') : foldProse(segment));
   192	  }
   193	  return out.join('').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
   194	}
   195	
   196	function foldProse(text: string): string {
   197	  let folded = text.replace(INVISIBLE, '').replace(/[’ʼ]/g, "'");
   198	  // Emphasis nests (`**bold *and italic* **`), so run to a fixed point rather
   199	  // than once. Bounded: every pass removes at least two characters.
   200	  for (let previous = ''; previous !== folded; ) {
   201	    previous = folded;
   202	    folded = folded.replace(PAIRED_EMPHASIS, '$2');
   203	  }
   204	  return folded.replace(MARKDOWN_LINK, (_match, label: string, destination: string) => {
   205	    const text = label.trim();
   206	    const target = destination.trim();
   207	    if (target.length === 0) return text;
   208	    // The destination disappears only when the text already states it, so an
   209	    // autolink does not turn into a stutter that nothing can bear.
   210	    return text === target ? text : `${text} ${target}`;
   211	  });
   212	}
   213	
   214	/**
   215	 * The lossy normalization, for the questions where being lossy is the safe
   580	export function quoteCoversOwnText(
   581	  quote: string,
   582	  ownText: string,
   583	  policy: ReceiptPolicy = RECEIPT_POLICY,
   584	): boolean {
   585	  const wanted = significant(quote, policy);
   586	  if (wanted.length === 0) return false;
   587	  return sameRun(significant(ownText, policy), wanted);
   588	}
   589	
   590	/**
   591	 * **Is this text offered as an assertion?**
   592	 *
   593	 * A question quoted verbatim is an OpenQuestion or a referral, never a Claim.
   594	 * Until r5 nothing asked: `objects.ts` requires a nonempty string and the
   595	 * receipt proves string equality, so `"Would we deploy production Friday?"`
   596	 * minted as a `claim` with an identical quote auto-accepted — the receipt was
   597	 * perfect and the reading turned somebody's question into their position.
   598	 *
   599	 * The test is the mark, and it is the same argument `RECEIPT_POLICY`'s
   600	 * `droppableTokens` makes for keeping `?` out of the set it will forgive:
   601	 * *"Bob will deploy Friday?" is a question and minting it as an assertion is the
   602	 * same defect in different clothes.* Here the mark is read rather than compared.
   603	 *
   604	 * Deliberately only the mark. An interrogative without one ("I wonder whether we
   605	 * ship Friday") reads as an assertion to any string check, and inventing a
   606	 * grammar to catch it would be the guess this file refuses to make elsewhere.
   607	 * What is claimed is exactly what is checked: **a sentence carrying a question
   608	 * mark is not an assertion.**
   609	 */
   610	export function isAssertion(text: string): boolean {
   611	  return !orderedTokens(text).includes('?');
   612	}
   410	  }
   411	
   412	  // ── The receipt, before anything else ────────────────────────────────────
   413	  //
   414	  // A reading whose citation is wrong is worse than no reading: the citation is
   415	  // what a person clicks to check it, and a wrong one survives casual review.
   416	  // The spike's worst output — a claim at 0.98 confidence attributed to a man
   417	  // who never said it — fails here and nowhere else.
   418	  //
   419	  // Only `reject`-severity problems discard. A `reclassify` one — an owner who
   420	  // did not write the message bearing the sentence — is not a defect in the
   421	  // reading, it *is* the third-party commitment case, and it routes below
   422	  // rather than dying here.
   423	  const problems: readonly ProvenanceProblem[] =
   424	    messages && messages.length > 0
   425	      ? validateProposalProvenance(
   426	          {
   427	            type: proposal.type,
   428	            provenance: proposal.provenance,
   429	            quote: proposal.quote,
   430	            proposer: proposal.proposer,
   431	            attributedTo,
   432	            // The sentence being staged. Without it the quote can be verbatim,
   433	            // correctly attributed, and about something else entirely — r2's
   434	            // gauntlet, major 1.
   435	            statement: text,
   436	          },
   437	          messages,
   438	        )
   439	      : [];
   440	  const rejecting = rejectingProblems(problems);
   441	  if (rejecting.length > 0) {
   442	    return {
   443	      ...base,
   444	      verdict: 'discard',
   445	      visibility: 'none',
   446	      rule: 'provenance_failed',
   447	      reason: `demoted below θ_min: ${rejecting.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   448	    };
   449	  }
   450	
   451	  // ── Already in the room ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   452	  const duplicate = context.acceptedObjects
   453	    ? findDuplicate(proposal.type, text, proposal.provenance, context.acceptedObjects)
   454	    : null;
   455	  if (duplicate) {
   456	    return {
   457	      ...base,
   458	      verdict: 'discard',
   459	      visibility: 'none',
   460	      rule: 'duplicate_of_accepted',
   461	      duplicateOf: duplicate.objectId,
   462	      reason: `the room already accepted "${duplicate.objectId}" from the same messages, saying the same thing`,
   463	    };
   464	  }
   465	
   466	  // ── A person staged this ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
   467	  //
   468	  // θ does not apply. #4's thresholds calibrate *extraction* confidence, and a
   469	  // person's self-report is not that number — it is not a number at all. A
   470	  // human-staged reading goes to the room to be judged, which is what staging
   471	  // means. (A person writing a fact outright uses `answerBinding` or direct
   472	  // acceptance; they do not need a proposal.)
   473	  if (proposal.proposer.kind === 'human') {
   474	    // …with one exception that is not about θ at all: a person naming *somebody
   475	    // else* on a commitment is still somebody else's sentence, and #4's rule
   476	    // does not care whether a machine or a colleague wrote it. The named owner
   477	    // is the one asked, and that is what the attention panel reads to decide
   478	    // whose confirm this is.
   479	    const staged = proposal.proposer.userId;
   480	    const namesAnother =
   481	      proposal.type === 'commitment' && attributedTo !== null && attributedTo !== staged;
   482	    return {
   483	      ...base,
   484	      verdict: 'pending',
   485	      visibility: 'needs_you',
   486	      attribution: proposal.type === 'commitment' ? (namesAnother ? 'third_party' : 'self') : null,
   487	      awaitingConfirmFrom: namesAnother ? attributedTo : null,
   488	      rule: 'human_proposer',
   489	      reason: namesAnother
   490	        ? `staged by user "${staged}", who named "${attributedTo}" as owner — it waits for "${attributedTo}" to confirm; nobody gets committed by someone else's sentence (#4), whoever wrote it`
   491	        : `staged by user "${staged}" — a person's reading goes to the room to be accepted, not through θ`,
   492	    };
   493	  }
   494	
   495	  const attribution =
   496	    proposal.type === 'commitment'
   497	      ? commitmentAttribution(attributedTo ?? '', proposal.provenance, messages, proposal.quote)
   498	      : null;
   499	
   500	  // ── The receipt this check declines to rule on ───────────────────────────
   501	  //
   502	  // **Above the θ_min discard since r5, and the two cases are different
   503	  // questions rather than two heights on one scale.**
   504	  //
   505	  // r4 put this below the discard and defended it: a weak reading should be
   506	  // dropped rather than promoted into the panel by a receipt problem. That
   507	  // argument is right about *one* input and r4 applied it to two. θ_min answers
   508	  // "is this reading worth somebody's attention" — a property of the model's own
   509	  // self-report, and a weak self-report with a clean receipt is noise, so it is
   510	  // still discarded below. A `refer` answers something else entirely: **the
   511	  // receipt and the statement disagree, and the disagreement was detected.**
   512	  // That is evidence about the record, not weakness in the reading.
   513	  //
   514	  // The input that made the difference visible: quote "Bob will not deploy
   515	  // production Friday", statement "Bob will deploy production Friday",
   516	  // confidence 0.4999. r4 discarded it as too weak to show — destroying the
   517	  // exact discrepancy the receipt exists to make visible, one thousandth of a
   518	  // point below a line that has nothing to do with what was found. The
   519	  // inversion is not less real for having been proposed timidly.
   520	  //
   521	  // Still **above everything that can accept**, so there is no path from here to
   522	  // `auto_accept`.
   523	  const referring = referringProblems(problems);
   524	  if (referring.length > 0) {
   525	    return {
   526	      ...base,
   527	      attribution,
   528	      verdict: 'pending',
   529	      visibility: 'quiet',
   530	      rule: 'receipt_not_certifiable',
   531	      reason: `not accepted on a machine's word, and not thrown away: ${referring.map((problem) => problem.detail).join('; ')}`,
   532	    };
   533	  }
   534	
   535	  // ── Below the discard line ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   536	  //
   537	  // Reached only with a receipt nothing found fault with, which is what makes
   538	  // discarding safe: there is no detected discrepancy here to destroy, only a
   539	  // model that is not convinced by its own reading.
   540	  if (proposal.confidence < rule.thetaMin) {
   541	    return {
   542	      ...base,
   543	      attribution,
   544	      verdict: 'discard',
   545	      visibility: 'none',

Found five substantive failures in committed `HEAD` (tested against `packages/core/dist`).

- **Blocking — code literals are auto-accepted as claims.**  
  Input: message, quote, and claim statement all equal `` `Deploy production Friday, regardless of CI status.` ``; model confidence `0.99`, claimant is the message author.  
  Observed: provenance problems `[]`; engine `auto_accept`; reducer creates the object with no issues. This contradicts the source’s own rule that a code span is “mention, not use.” The code preserves backticks but never rejects a claim whose entire asserted statement is code.  
  `packages/core/src/matching.ts:154-162`, `packages/core/src/escalation.ts:1002-1009`  
  The new tests only try a quote with delimiters but a statement without them, so they miss this exact all-verbatim path: `packages/core/test/residue.test.ts:155-194`.

- **Blocking — non-ASCII question marks mint claims.**  
  Input: message/quote/statement `Will we deploy production Friday？` (U+FF1F), type `claim`, confidence `0.99`.  
  Observed: `isAssertion(...) === true`, provenance problems `[]`, engine `auto_accept`; reducer accepts the claim.  
  The tokenizer retains `？`, but `isAssertion` checks only ASCII `?`. This violates the stated “a statement carrying a question mark” rule.  
  `packages/core/src/matching.ts:610-611`

- **Blocking — a later correction becomes invisible after 200 messages.**  
  Exact constructed window:
  ```js
  [
    { id: 'm0', authorId: 'user_alice', body: 'Alice will deploy production Friday.' },
    ...Array.from({ length: 200 }, (_, i) => ({
      id: `f${i + 1}`, authorId: 'user_bob',
      body: `Unrelated status update number ${i + 1}.`,
    })),
    { id: 'm201', authorId: 'user_alice',
      body: 'Correction: Alice will not deploy production Friday.' },
  ]
  ```
  The model cites/quotes `m0` and proposes the matching claim at `0.99`.  
  Observed: `laterRevision(...) === null`; engine `auto_accept`.  
  This is exactly a documented “safe” miss defended in a comment: the scanner silently stops at `maxLaterMessagesScanned`, then certifies despite not reading the correction.  
  `packages/core/src/escalation.ts:1145-1154`, `packages/core/src/policy.ts:319-323`

- **Major — deduplication silently destroys a detected receipt discrepancy before the new `refer` ordering runs.**  
  Input: accepted ref `{ objectId: 'old', type: 'claim', text: 'Alice will deploy production Friday.', messageIds: ['m'] }`; new model proposal has the same statement/provenance but quote/source `Alice will not deploy production Friday.` and confidence `0.99`.  
  Observed: without `acceptedObjects`, verdict is `pending / receipt_not_certifiable`; with that accepted ref, verdict becomes `discard / duplicate_of_accepted`.  
  `findDuplicate` runs before `referringProblems`, so a known dropped `not` is deleted rather than surfaced. This defeats the claim that a detected discrepancy is never destroyed.  
  `packages/core/src/acceptance.ts:451-464`, `packages/core/src/acceptance.ts:523-533`

- **Major — receipt normalization changes code-literal content.**  
  Input source: `Set the deployment password to \`a  b\` immediately.`  
  Quote/statement: `Set the deployment password to \`a b\` immediately.`  
  Observed: both normalize to the same string; provenance passes and engine returns `auto_accept`.  
  The two code literals are distinct values, while the round claims code spans survive intact. Final whitespace collapsing applies after protected code spans are rejoined.  
  `packages/core/src/matching.ts:186-194`

I found no separate failure in the model-only type gates, `hasContent`, or the stated shared-validator enumeration. The authority/reducer documentation’s independent-gate list matches those acceptance-path checks.
